270	/**
271	 * Resolve the JavaScript runtime used by PolyglotExecutor.
272	 *
273	 * PR #190 (f69b0d2) made `process.execPath` the default so snap-Node
274	 * envs would not re-invoke the snap wrapper via PATH. That assumed
275	 * `process.execPath` always points at a JS runtime — true on Node,
276	 * tsx, and snap-Node, but FALSE when context-mode runs in-process
277	 * inside a bun-compiled self-contained binary (OpenCode, Kilo, …).
278	 * In those hosts, `process.execPath` resolves to `opencode.exe` /
279	 * `opencode` (NOT node), and spawning that with a `.js` argument
280	 * triggers the yargs "Failed to change directory" error (#731).
281	 *
282	 * Fix: gate `process.execPath` on the existing `JS_RUNTIMES`
283	 * allowlist (single source of truth — same set used by
284	 * `buildNodeCommand()` in src/adapters/types.ts since PR #708). When
285	 * the execPath basename is not a known JS runtime, fall back to a
286	 * PATH-resolved `node`. If neither is reachable, return `null` and
287	 * let ctx_doctor surface an actionable error.
288	 *
289	 * The cross-OS guard is the allowlist itself — NOT a `win32` check.
290	 * OpenCode ships self-contained binaries on macOS and Linux too,
291	 * and the bug reproduces identically there.
292	 */
293	export function resolveJavascriptRuntime(
294	  bun: string | null,
295	  deps: {
296	    execPath?: string;
297	    commandExists?: (cmd: string) => boolean;
298	  } = {},
299	): string | null {
300	  if (bun) return bun;
301	
302	  const execPath = deps.execPath ?? process.execPath;
303	  const cmdExists = deps.commandExists ?? commandExists;
304	
305	  // Cross-OS basename: split on either separator, strip optional `.exe`.
306	  const base = execPath
307	    .split(/[\\/]/)
308	    .pop()!
309	    .replace(/\.exe$/i, "");
310	
311	  if (JS_RUNTIMES.has(base)) {
312	    // Real JS runtime (node, bun, deno) — preserves #190 snap-Node fix
313	    // because the snap wrapper's binary is literally named `node`.
314	    //
315	    // Issue #800 — liveness guard: on Homebrew, process.execPath points into
316	    // the versioned Cellar (/opt/homebrew/Cellar/node/26.0.0/bin/node).
317	    // `brew upgrade` + `brew cleanup` deletes the old Cellar, so the path
318	    // dangles for the life of the already-running MCP server.  If the path
319	    // doesn't exist on disk, skip it and fall through to PATH node.
320	    if (existsSync(execPath)) {
321	      return execPath;
322	    }
323	    // Stale execPath (deleted Cellar, corrupted install, uninstall while
324	    // process alive).  Fall through to PATH resolution below.
325	  }
326	
327	  // Host binary (opencode/kilo/etc.) — fall back to node on PATH.
328	  if (cmdExists("node")) return "node";
329	
330	  // No usable runtime — doctor + summary must handle null gracefully.
331	  return null;
332	}
333	
334	export function detectRuntimes(): RuntimeMap {
335	  const hasBun = bunExists();
336	  const bun = hasBun ? bunCommand() : null;
337	
338	  // Honor SHELL env var when it points at a real binary AND the basename is
339	  // an allowlisted shell. Lets users with non-standard setups (custom bash,
340	  // msys2, pwsh) pin context-mode to their preferred shell.
341	  //
342	  // Allowlist (PR #401 ops review): basename must match
343	  // /^(bash|sh|zsh|dash|pwsh|powershell|cmd)(\.exe)?$/. Without this guard, an attacker
344	  // who controls SHELL (e.g., supply-chain compromise of a profile script)
345	  // could redirect the executor to /usr/bin/python or any arbitrary binary.
346	  const userShell = process.env.SHELL;
347	  const isWin = process.platform === "win32";
348	  const windowsBash = isWin ? resolveWindowsBash() : null;
349	  const shellOverride = userShell &&
350	    existsSync(userShell) &&
351	    isAllowlistedShell(userShell) &&
352	    !(isWin && isWindowsWslBash(userShell)) &&
353	    // Windows OpenSSH can inject the system cmd.exe as ambient SHELL. When
354	    // Git Bash is installed, treating that as an explicit override breaks the
355	    // POSIX shell executor path restored by #36/#384/#791.
356	    !(isWin && windowsBash && isWindowsSystemCmd(userShell))
357	    ? userShell
358	    : null;
359	
360	  return {
361	    javascript: resolveJavascriptRuntime(bun),
362	    typescript: bun
363	      ? bun
364	      : commandExists("tsx")
365	        ? "tsx"
366	        : commandExists("ts-node")
367	          ? "ts-node"
368	          : null,
369	    python: runnableExists("python3")
370	      ? "python3"
371	      : runnableExists("python")
372	        ? "python"
373	        : runnableExists("py")
374	          ? "py"
375	          : null,
376	    shell: shellOverride ?? (isWin
377	      ? resolveWindowsShell(windowsBash)
378	      : commandExists("bash") ? "bash" : "sh"),
379	    ruby: commandExists("ruby") ? "ruby" : null,
380	    go: commandExists("go") ? "go" : null,
381	    rust: commandExists("rustc") ? "rustc" : null,
382	    php: commandExists("php") ? "php" : null,
383	    perl: commandExists("perl") ? "perl" : null,
384	    r: commandExists("Rscript")
385	      ? "Rscript"
386	      : commandExists("r")
387	        ? "r"
388	        : null,
389	    elixir: commandExists("elixir") ? "elixir" : null,
390	    csharp: commandExists("dotnet-script") ? "dotnet-script" : null,
391	  };
392	}
393	
394	export function hasBunRuntime(): boolean {
395	  return bunExists();
396	}
397	
398	/**
399	 * Resolved JS runtime for hook spawn commands. `path` is the absolute (or
400	 * bare-name on POSIX where PATH resolution is reliable) binary path.
401	 * `isBun` is true only when we successfully probed a Bun ≥1.0 install.
402	 */
403	export interface HookRuntime {
404	  readonly path: string;
405	  readonly isBun: boolean;
406	}
407	
408	/**
409	 * Cached result of {@link resolveHookRuntime}. Populated on first call so the
410	 * relatively expensive `bun --version` probe runs at most once per process.
411	 * Reset via {@link resetHookRuntimeCache} (test-only).
412	 */
413	let _hookRuntimeCache: HookRuntime | null = null;
414	
415	/**
416	 * Reset the hook-runtime resolution cache. Test-only — production code
417	 * should never call this. Vitest mocks `node:child_process`/`node:fs`
418	 * per-test, so the per-process cache from a previous test would otherwise
419	 * mask the mock and yield the host's real bun/node detection result.
420	 */
421	export function resetHookRuntimeCache(): void {
422	  _hookRuntimeCache = null;
423	}
424	
425	/**
426	 * Parse a `bun --version` stdout string and return true when the version is
427	 * ≥1.0.0. Anything that doesn't match `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (with optional
428	 * pre-release suffix) returns false — we refuse to trust runtimes whose
429	 * version we can't read because the failure mode is silent miscompare
430	 * (e.g. a banner line getting interpreted as "0.0.0").
431	 */
432	function bunVersionAtLeast1(versionOutput: string): boolean {
433	  const trimmed = versionOutput.trim();
434	  const m = /^(\d+)\.(\d+)\.(\d+)/.exec(trimmed);
435	  if (!m) return false;
436	  const major = Number(m[1]);
437	  return Number.isFinite(major) && major >= 1;
438	}
439	
440	/**
441	 * Resolve the JS runtime to use for spawning hook scripts (issue #738).
442	 *
443	 * Returns Bun when:
444	 *   - a bun binary is located via {@link bunCommand} (already handles the
445	 *     Windows .cmd shim trap from #506 + absolute path fallbacks), AND
446	 *   - `bun --version` exits 0 within the probe timeout, AND
447	 *   - the reported semver major is ≥1.
448	 *
449	 * Returns Node (`process.execPath`) on every other path — missing bun,
450	 * version probe failure, version <1, malformed version banner. Silent
451	 * fallback: never throws, never logs to stderr (a noisy log would clutter
452	 * the same MCP boot output that #719 tightened up).
453	 *
454	 * Result is cached at module load so the cost is amortised across every
455	 * hook command emission for the lifetime of the process. The cache also
456	 * keeps the behaviour deterministic — if the user `brew uninstall bun`
457	 * mid-session, the cached resolution stays valid for that session and the
458	 * next MCP boot re-detects.
459	 *
460	 * Why bun ≥1.0 instead of "any bun":
461	 *   - Bun 0.x had multiple ESM/module-resolution regressions that broke
462	 *     dynamic `import()` inside hooks (and our hooks do ~7 dynamic imports
463	 *     in `pretooluse.mjs`).
464	 *   - 1.0 ships stable npm-compat that our better-sqlite3-adjacent code
465	 *     relies on indirectly (hooks share `ensure-deps.mjs` which is
466	 *     bun-safe past 1.0 but not 0.x).
467	 *
468	 * NOT used by:
469	 *   - `buildNodeCommand` — kept on `process.execPath` for openclaw doctor /
470	 *     upgrade hints which must invoke the better-sqlite3-loading CLI on
471	 *     Node (#543: bun cannot dlopen better-sqlite3's prebuilt .node).
472	 *   - `ensure-deps.mjs` — separate path, must stay on Node for the same
473	 *     reason.
474	 *   - `ctx_upgrade` — separate path, must stay on Node for the same reason.
475	 */
476	/**
477	 * Liveness-guarded Node path for the hook-runtime fallback (issue #841).
478	 *
479	 * `process.execPath` is pinned into every baked hook command because PATH
480	 * resolution is unreliable for hooks (#190 snap-Node re-invokes the wrapper;
481	 * #369 Windows Git Bash / MSYS can't resolve a bare `node`). But under a
482	 * version manager (mise / asdf / nvm) execPath is a *version-pinned* absolute
483	 * path — e.g. `~/.local/share/mise/installs/node/20.1.0/bin/node`. A routine
484	 * `mise upgrade node` installs the next patch and DELETES the 20.1.0 dir, so
485	 * the cached path dangles and every hook spawn fails with ENOENT — silently
486	 * killing context-mode for that user.
487	 *
488	 * Same liveness-guard shape as the #800/#803 fix in
489	 * {@link resolveJavascriptRuntime}: use the pinned execPath IFF it still
490	 * exists on disk (preserving the #190/#369 reasons it was pinned), otherwise
491	 * re-resolve a working `node` from PATH. The version manager's shim dir is on
492	 * PATH and always points at the current patch, so bare `node` heals the host
493	 * without a re-install. Falls back to the (stale) execPath only when no PATH
494	 * node is reachable either — a strictly-better last resort than a dangling
495	 * versioned path, and the doctor/upgrade flows surface the actionable error.
496	 */
497	function liveNodeRuntime(): HookRuntime {
498	  if (existsSync(process.execPath)) {
499	    return { path: process.execPath, isBun: false };
500	  }
501	  if (commandExists("node")) {
502	    return { path: "node", isBun: false };
503	  }
504	  return { path: process.execPath, isBun: false };
505	}
506	
507	export function resolveHookRuntime(): HookRuntime {
508	  if (_hookRuntimeCache) return _hookRuntimeCache;
509	  const nodeFallback: HookRuntime = liveNodeRuntime();
510	  try {
511	    if (!bunExists()) {
512	      _hookRuntimeCache = nodeFallback;
513	      return _hookRuntimeCache;
514	    }
515	    const bun = bunCommand();
516	    // Re-use the same probe shape as getVersion (POSIX execFile, Windows
517	    // execSync quoted string for DEP0190 compliance).
518	    let versionOutput: string;
519	    try {
520	      if (process.platform === "win32") {
521	        const out = execSync(`"${bun}" --version`, {
522	          encoding: "utf-8",
523	          stdio: ["pipe", "pipe", "pipe"],
524	          timeout: 5000,
525	        });
526	        versionOutput = String(out);
527	      } else {
528	        const out = execFileSync(bun, ["--version"], {
529	          encoding: "utf-8",
530	          stdio: ["pipe", "pipe", "pipe"],
531	          timeout: 5000,
532	        });
533	        versionOutput = String(out);
534	      }
535	    } catch {
536	      _hookRuntimeCache = nodeFallback;
537	      return _hookRuntimeCache;
538	    }
539	    if (!bunVersionAtLeast1(versionOutput)) {
540	      _hookRuntimeCache = nodeFallback;
541	      return _hookRuntimeCache;
542	    }
543	    _hookRuntimeCache = { path: bun, isBun: true };
544	    return _hookRuntimeCache;
545	  } catch {
546	    _hookRuntimeCache = nodeFallback;
547	    return _hookRuntimeCache;
548	  }
549	}

551	export function getRuntimeSummary(runtimes: RuntimeMap): string {
552	  const lines: string[] = [];
553	  const bunPreferred = runtimes.javascript?.endsWith("bun") ?? false;
554	
555	  if (runtimes.javascript) {

558	    );

561	    // Surface actionable guidance instead of rendering literal `null`.
562	    lines.push(

564	    );
565	  }
566

568	    lines.push(
569	      `  TypeScript: ${runtimes.typescript} (${getVersion(runtimes.typescript)})`,
570	    );
571	  } else {
572	    lines.push(
573	      `  TypeScript: not available (install bun, tsx, or ts-node)`,
574	    );
575	  }
576	
577	  if (runtimes.python) {
578	    lines.push(
579	      `  Python:     ${runtimes.python} (${getVersion(runtimes.python)})`,
580	    );
581	  } else {
582	    lines.push(`  Python:     not available`);
583	  }
584

586	    `  Shell:      ${runtimes.shell} (${getVersion(runtimes.shell)})`,
587	  );

590	  if (runtimes.ruby)

593	    );
594	  if (runtimes.go)

596	  if (runtimes.rust)
597	    lines.push(

599	    );

601	    lines.push(
602	      `  PHP:        ${runtimes.php} (${getVersion(runtimes.php)})`,
603	    );
604	  if (runtimes.perl)
605	    lines.push(
606	      `  Perl:       ${runtimes.perl} (${getVersion(runtimes.perl)})`,
607	    );
608	  if (runtimes.r)
609	    lines.push(`  R:          ${runtimes.r} (${getVersion(runtimes.r)})`);
610	  if (runtimes.elixir)
611	    lines.push(
612	      `  Elixir:     ${runtimes.elixir} (${getVersion(runtimes.elixir)})`,
613	    );
614	  if (runtimes.csharp)
615	    lines.push(
616	      `  C#:         ${runtimes.csharp} (${getVersion(runtimes.csharp)})`,
617	    );
618	
619	  if (!bunPreferred) {
620	    lines.push("");
621	    lines.push(
622	      "  Tip: Install Bun for 3-5x faster JS/TS execution → https://bun.sh",
623	    );
624	  }
625

627	}

629	export function getAvailableLanguages(runtimes: RuntimeMap): Language[] {

638	  if (runtimes.r) langs.push("r");

644	export function buildCommand(
645	  runtimes: RuntimeMap,
646	  language: Language,
647	  filePath: string,
648	): string[] {
649	  switch (language) {
650	    case "javascript":
651	      if (!runtimes.javascript) {
652	        // #731: in-process plugin host (opencode/kilo binary) AND no
653	        // PATH-resolvable node. Refuse early with an actionable error
654	        // instead of spawning the host binary (the original bug shape).
655	        throw new Error(
656	          "No JavaScript runtime available. Install Node.js or Bun on PATH (the host process is not itself a JS runtime).",
657	        );
658	      }
659	      return BUN_BASENAME.test(runtimeBasename(runtimes.javascript))
660	        ? [runtimes.javascript, "run", filePath]
661	        : [runtimes.javascript, filePath];
662	
663	    case "typescript":
664	      if (!runtimes.typescript) {
665	        throw new Error(
666	          "No TypeScript runtime available. Install one of: bun (recommended), tsx (npm i -g tsx), or ts-node.",
667	        );
668	      }
669	      if (BUN_BASENAME.test(runtimeBasename(runtimes.typescript))) return [runtimes.typescript, "run", filePath];
670	      if (runtimes.typescript === "tsx") return ["tsx", filePath];
671	      return ["ts-node", filePath];
672	
673	    case "python":
674	      if (!runtimes.python) {
675	        throw new Error(
676	          "No Python runtime available. Install python3 or python.",
677	        );
678	      }
679	      return [runtimes.python, filePath];
680	
681	    case "shell": {
682	      // Re-evaluate platform per call so detection-time and command-build-time
683	      // can be tested independently (and to allow tests to stub process.platform).
684	      const winNow = process.platform === "win32";
685	      if (winNow) {
686	        const shellName = runtimes.shell.toLowerCase();
687	        if (shellName.includes("bash") || shellName.endsWith("/sh") || shellName.endsWith("\\sh.exe")) {
688	          // bash -c "source 'path'" — avoids MSYS2 path mangling on non-C:
689	          // drives. When bash.exe receives a script as a direct argument,
690	          // MSYS rewrites D:\tmp\script → D:\c\tmp\script and execution
691	          // breaks. The -c flag prevents MSYS from touching the file arg.
692	          // Single-quote escape: ' → '\''
693	          const escaped = filePath.replace(/'/g, "'\\''");
694	          return [runtimes.shell, "-c", `source '${escaped}'`];
695	        }
696	        if (shellName.includes("powershell") || shellName.includes("pwsh")) {
697	          // Windows PowerShell defaults to Restricted when no execution policy
698	          // is configured. Use process-scoped Bypass so generated temp scripts
699	          // run without changing machine/user policy.
700	          return [runtimes.shell, "-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", filePath];
701	        }
702	        const shellBase = shellName.split(/[\\/]/).pop() ?? shellName;
703	        if (shellBase === "cmd" || shellBase === "cmd.exe") {
704	          return [runtimes.shell, "/d", "/s", "/c", filePath];
705	        }
706	        // Other Windows shells: direct file.
707	      }
708	      return [runtimes.shell, filePath];
709	    }
710	
711	    case "ruby":
712	      if (!runtimes.ruby) {
713	        throw new Error("Ruby not available. Install ruby.");
714	      }
715	      return [runtimes.ruby, filePath];
716	
717	    case "go":
718	      if (!runtimes.go) {
719	        throw new Error("Go not available. Install go.");
720	      }
721	      return ["go", "run", filePath];
722	
723	    case "rust": {
724	      if (!runtimes.rust) {
725	        throw new Error(
726	          "Rust not available. Install rustc via https://rustup.rs",
727	        );
728	      }
729	      // Rust needs compile + run — handled specially in executor
730	      return ["__rust_compile_run__", filePath];
731	    }
732	
733	    case "php":
734	      if (!runtimes.php) {
735	        throw new Error("PHP not available. Install php.");
736	      }
737	      return ["php", filePath];
738	
739	    case "perl":
740	      if (!runtimes.perl) {
741	        throw new Error("Perl not available. Install perl.");
742	      }
743	      return ["perl", filePath];
744	
745	    case "r":
746	      if (!runtimes.r) {
747	        throw new Error("R not available. Install R / Rscript.");
748	      }
749	      return [runtimes.r, filePath];
750	
751	    case "elixir":
752	      if (!runtimes.elixir) {
753	        throw new Error( "Elixir not available. Install elixir.");
754	      }
755	      return ["elixir", filePath];
756	
757	    case "csharp":
758	      if (!runtimes.csharp) {
759	        throw new Error(
760	          "C# not available. Install dotnet-script via `dotnet tool install -g dotnet-script`.",
761	        );
762	      }
763	      return [runtimes.csharp, filePath];
764	  }
765	}

tool_result 6,196 chars
1	/**
2	 * truncate — Pure string truncation and escaping utilities for context-mode.
3	 *
4	 * These helpers are used by the core ContentStore (chunking) and
5	 * SessionDB (snapshot building). They are extracted here so any
6	 * consumer can import them without pulling in the full store or executor.
7	 */
8	
9	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
10	// Internal: byte-safe prefix
11	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
12	
13	/**
14	 * Return the longest character-prefix of `str` whose UTF-8 encoding is at
15	 * most `maxBytes` bytes. Uses binary search to avoid O(n²) scanning. Returns
16	 * "" when `maxBytes` is <= 0 so callers never exceed their budget.
17	 *
18	 * Guards against splitting a UTF-16 surrogate pair: if the prefix would end
19	 * on a lone high surrogate, back off one code unit so the result round-trips
20	 * through UTF-8 without producing a U+FFFD replacement character.
21	 */
22	function byteSafePrefix(str: string, maxBytes: number): string {
23	  if (maxBytes <= 0) return "";
24	  if (Buffer.byteLength(str) <= maxBytes) return str;
25	
26	  let lo = 0;
27	  let hi = str.length;
28	  while (lo < hi) {
29	    const mid = (lo + hi + 1) >> 1;
30	    if (Buffer.byteLength(str.slice(0, mid)) <= maxBytes) {
31	      lo = mid;
32	    } else {
33	      hi = mid - 1;
34	    }
35	  }
36	
37	  // If we landed between a high and low surrogate, back off so the prefix
38	  // ends on a valid code point boundary.
39	  if (lo > 0) {
40	    const code = str.charCodeAt(lo - 1);
41	    if (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdbff) lo -= 1;
42	  }
43	  return str.slice(0, lo);
44	}
45	
46	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
47	// Char-count prefix (UTF-16 surrogate-safe)
48	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
49	
50	/**
51	 * Return a prefix of `str` no longer than `maxChars` UTF-16 code units, with
52	 * the cut backed off by one unit if it would split a surrogate pair. Mirrors
53	 * the surrogate-safety semantics of the internal `byteSafePrefix`, but uses
54	 * a character budget rather than a byte budget.
55	 *
56	 * Use this instead of bare `str.slice(0, n)` whenever the result is later
57	 * JSON-encoded for an RFC 8259-strict consumer (e.g. tool_result payloads
58	 * sent back to the host LLM API). `JSON.stringify` will emit a lone high
59	 * surrogate as a literal `\uD8xx` escape, which is invalid JSON.
60	 *
61	 * @param str      - Input string.
62	 * @param maxChars - Hard cap in UTF-16 code units.
63	 */
64	export function charSafePrefix(str: string, maxChars: number): string {
65	  if (maxChars <= 0) return "";
66	  if (str.length <= maxChars) return str;
67	
68	  let end = maxChars;
69	  const code = str.charCodeAt(end - 1);
70	  if (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdbff) end -= 1;
71	  return str.slice(0, end);
72	}
73	
74	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
75	// JSON truncation
76	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
77	
78	/**
79	 * Serialize a value to JSON, then truncate the result to `maxBytes` bytes.
80	 * If truncation occurs, the string is cut at a UTF-8-safe boundary and
81	 * "... [truncated]" is appended. The result is NOT guaranteed to be valid
82	 * JSON after truncation — it is suitable only for display/logging.
83	 *
84	 * The returned string is always <= `maxBytes` bytes. When `maxBytes` is
85	 * smaller than the marker, the marker itself is byte-safely truncated.
86	 *
87	 * @param value    - Any JSON-serializable value.
88	 * @param maxBytes - Maximum byte length of the returned string.
89	 * @param indent   - JSON indentation spaces (default 2). Pass 0 for compact.
90	 */
91	export function truncateJSON(
92	  value: unknown,
93	  maxBytes: number,
94	  indent: number = 2,
95	): string {
96	  const serialized = JSON.stringify(value, null, indent) ?? "null";
97	  if (Buffer.byteLength(serialized) <= maxBytes) return serialized;
98	
99	  const marker = "... [truncated]";
100	  const markerBytes = Buffer.byteLength(marker);
101	
102	  // Degenerate budget: can't fit serialized content + marker. Fit as much of
103	  // the marker as we can so the return still honors `maxBytes`.
104	  if (maxBytes <= markerBytes) return byteSafePrefix(marker, maxBytes);
105	
106	  return byteSafePrefix(serialized, maxBytes - markerBytes) + marker;
107	}
108	
109	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
110	// XML / HTML escaping
111	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
112	
113	/**
114	 * Escape a string for safe embedding in an XML or HTML attribute or text node.
115	 * Replaces the five XML-reserved characters: `&`, `<`, `>`, `"`, `'`.
116	 *
117	 * Used by the resume snapshot template builder to embed user content in
118	 * `<tool_response>` and `<user_message>` XML tags without breaking the
119	 * structured prompt format.
120	 */
121	export function escapeXML(str: string): string {
122	  return str
123	    .replace(/&/g, "&amp;")
124	    .replace(/</g, "&lt;")
125	    .replace(/>/g, "&gt;")
126	    .replace(/"/g, "&quot;")
127	    .replace(/'/g, "&apos;");
128	}
129	
130	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
131	// maxBytes guard
132	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
133	
134	/**
135	 * Return `str` unchanged if it fits within `maxBytes`, otherwise return a
136	 * byte-safe slice with an ellipsis appended. Useful for single-value fields
137	 * (e.g., tool response strings) where head+tail splitting is not needed.
138	 *
139	 * The returned string is always <= `maxBytes` bytes. When `maxBytes` is
140	 * smaller than the ellipsis marker, the marker itself is byte-safely truncated.
141	 *
142	 * @param str      - Input string.
143	 * @param maxBytes - Hard byte cap.
144	 */
145	export function capBytes(str: string, maxBytes: number): string {
146	  if (Buffer.byteLength(str) <= maxBytes) return str;
147	
148	  const marker = "...";
149	  const markerBytes = Buffer.byteLength(marker);
150	
151	  if (maxBytes <= markerBytes) return byteSafePrefix(marker, maxBytes);
152	
153	  return byteSafePrefix(str, maxBytes - markerBytes) + marker;
154	}

1	/**
2	 * Classify non-zero exit codes for ctx_execute / ctx_execute_file.
3	 *
4	 * Shell commands like `grep` exit 1 for "no matches" — not a real error.
5	 * We treat exit code 1 as a soft failure when:
6	 *   - language is "shell"
7	 *   - exit code is exactly 1
8	 *   - stdout has non-whitespace content
9	 */
10	export interface ExitClassification {
11	  isError: boolean;
12	  output: string;
13	}
14	
15	export function classifyNonZeroExit(params: {
16	  language: string;
17	  exitCode: number;
18	  stdout: string;
19	  stderr: string;
20	}): ExitClassification {
21	  const { language, exitCode, stdout, stderr } = params;
22	  const isSoftFail =
23	    language === "shell" &&
24	    exitCode === 1 &&
25	    stdout.trim().length > 0;
26	
27	  return {
28	    isError: !isSoftFail,
29	    output: isSoftFail
30	      ? stdout
31	      : `Exit code: ${exitCode}\n\nstdout:\n${stdout}\n\nstderr:\n${stderr}`,
32	  };
33	}

1	/**
2	 * Cache-key / storage-label composition for ctx_fetch_and_index.
3	 *
4	 * Two distinct URLs that share a user-supplied `source` label MUST NOT collide
5	 * in the cache (or in FTS5 storage, since indexing dedups by label). Compose
6	 * `${source}::${url}` whenever a `source` is explicitly provided so cache
7	 * lookup, dedup, and re-indexing are all per-(source,url). When no `source`
8	 * is provided the URL itself is the unique key — no composition needed.
9	 *
10	 * `ctx_search(source: "Docs")` continues to work because LIKE-mode source
11	 * filtering matches on the substring "Docs" inside "Docs::https://…".
12	 */
13	export function composeFetchCacheKey(source: string | undefined, url: string): string {
14	  return source === undefined ? url : `${source}::${url}`;
15	}

18	 * On Windows, shell scripts usually get NO extension to avoid Windows

24	  javascript: "js",

38	/** Pure helper — exported for unit testing. Returns "script" or "script.<ext>". */
39	export function buildScriptFilename(
40	  language: Language,
41	  platform: NodeJS.Platform,
42	  shellPath?: string | null,
43	): string {
44	  if (platform === "win32" && language === "shell") {
45	    const shellName = shellPath?.toLowerCase() ?? "";
46	    if (shellName.includes("powershell") || shellName.includes("pwsh")) return "script.ps1";
47	    const shellBase = shellName.split(/[\\/]/).pop() ?? shellName;
48	    if (shellBase === "cmd" || shellBase === "cmd.exe") return "script.cmd";
49	    return "script";
50	  }
51	  return `script.${SCRIPT_EXT[language]}`;
52	}

71	  platform: NodeJS.Platform,
72	): string {
73	  if (platform === "win32" || !inheritedPath) return code;
74	  return `export PATH=${quoteForPosixShell(inheritedPath)}\n${code}`;

77	function isPowerShell(shellPath: string | null | undefined): boolean {

82	export function buildPowerShellScriptContent(code: string): string {
83	  // Prefix a UTF-8 BOM so Windows PowerShell 5.1 reliably detects the script
84	  // file as UTF-8 (without it, 5.1 falls back to the ANSI code page and
85	  // mangles non-ASCII characters in the script body).
86	  return [
87	    "\uFEFF[Console]::InputEncoding = [System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new()",
88	    "[Console]::OutputEncoding = [System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new()",
89	    "$OutputEncoding = [System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new()",
90	    code,
91	  ].join("\n");
92	}

94	/**
95	 * Resolve the real OS temp directory, bypassing any TMPDIR env override.
96	 * os.tmpdir() reads TMPDIR from the environment, which some shells/tools
97	 * set to the project root — causing temp files to pollute the working tree.
98	 */
99	const OS_TMPDIR = (() => {
100	  if (isWin) return process.env.TEMP ?? process.env.TMP ?? tmpdir();
101	  try {
102	    const result = execFileSync(
103	      process.platform === "darwin" ? "getconf" : "mktemp",
104	      process.platform === "darwin" ? ["DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR"] : ["-u", "-d"],
105	      { env: { ...process.env, TMPDIR: undefined as unknown as string }, encoding: "utf-8" },
106	    ).trim();
107	    const dir = process.platform === "darwin" ? result : resolve(result, "..");
108	    if (dir && dir !== process.cwd()) return dir;
109	  } catch { /* fall through */ }
110	  return "/tmp";
111	})();

113	/**
114	 * Pure helper — exported for unit testing. Issue #782.
115	 *
116	 * On Windows, the sandbox shell runtime is Git Bash. A bare `mvn` invocation
117	 * runs Maven's POSIX shell script, which on the `mingw=true` branch (uname →
118	 * MINGW64_NT-*) fails to convert `CLASSWORLDS_JAR` from a POSIX path
119	 * (`/c/tools/maven/boot/plexus-classworlds-*.jar`) to a Windows path. Native
120	 * `java.exe` then can't resolve the bootstrap jar → ClassNotFoundException for
121	 * `org.codehaus.plexus.classworlds.launcher.Launcher`.
122	 *
123	 * The third-way fix (issue Option C): rewrite the bare `mvn` token to `mvn.cmd`,
124	 * the native Windows launcher that uses Windows-native paths and bypasses the
125	 * broken mingw shell branch entirely. This does NOT touch the global MSYS
126	 * path-conversion env (MSYS_NO_PATHCONV / MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL), which #826/#791
127	 * deliberately leave unset so native git.exe launched from bash keeps its
128	 * /tmp→C:\ argument conversion. Re-enabling global suppression would re-break
129	 * native git; rewriting only the mvn token keeps both correct.
130	 *
131	 * Only a `mvn` that starts a command (start of string, or after a shell
132	 * separator `&& | ; ( newline`) is rewritten. `mvnw`, `mvnd`, `mymvn`,
133	 * paths like `./mvnw`, and an already-`mvn.cmd` token are left untouched
134	 * (the token must be exactly `mvn` followed by whitespace or end-of-string).
135	 */
136	export function rewriteWindowsBuildTools(
137	  code: string,
138	  platform: NodeJS.Platform,
139	): string {
140	  if (platform !== "win32") return code;
141	  // Rewrite a bare `mvn` command token to `mvn.cmd` (Maven's native Windows launcher).
142	  // Algorithmic (no regex): only at a command-start position (string start or right
143	  // after a shell separator ; & | ( newline, skipping leading spaces/tabs) and only
144	  // when the token is exactly `mvn` followed by whitespace or end — leaves
145	  // mvnw / mvnd / ./mvnw / already-mvn.cmd untouched.
146	  const SEP = new Set([";", "&", "|", "(", "\n"]);
147	  let out = "";
148	  let atStart = true;
149	  let i = 0;
150	  while (i < code.length) {
151	    const ch = code[i];
152	    if (atStart && (ch === " " || ch === "\t")) {
153	      out += ch;
154	      i++;
155	      continue;
156	    }
157	    if (atStart && code.startsWith("mvn", i)) {
158	      const after = code[i + 3];
159	      if (after === undefined || after === " " || after === "\t" || after === "\n") {
160	        out += "mvn.cmd";
161	        i += 3;
162	        atStart = false;
163	        continue;
164	      }
165	    }
166	    out += ch;
167	    atStart = SEP.has(ch);
168	    i++;
169	  }
170	  return out;
171	}
172	
173	/**
174	 * Remove a sandbox temp dir, retrying on Windows. Issue #788.
175	 *
176	 * On Windows, a child process that opened SQLite databases inside the sandbox
177	 * can leave `*-wal` / `*-shm` files with handles that linger briefly after the
178	 * process exits. A single `rmSync` then throws EBUSY/EPERM/ENOTEMPTY and the
179	 * old silent `catch {}` swallowed it, leaking `.ctx-mode-*` directories under
180	 * `%TEMP%`. Node's `rmSync({ maxRetries, retryDelay })` is purpose-built for
181	 * exactly this Windows-handle race, so let it back off and retry.
182	 */
183	function cleanupTmpDir(tmpDir: string): void {
184	  try {
185	    rmSync(tmpDir, {
186	      recursive: true,
187	      force: true,
188	      maxRetries: isWin ? 8 : 2,
189	      retryDelay: 100,
190	    });
191	  } catch {
192	    /* best-effort — OS will reclaim %TEMP% eventually */
193	  }
194	}
195	
196	/** Kill process tree — on Windows uses taskkill /T; on Unix kills the process group. */
197	function killTree(proc: ReturnType<typeof spawn>): void {
198	  if (isWin && proc.pid) {
199	    try {
200	      execSync(`taskkill /F /T /PID ${proc.pid}`, { stdio: "pipe" });
201	    } catch { /* already dead */ }
202	  } else if (proc.pid) {
203	    try {
204	      // Kill entire process group (negative PID) to prevent orphaned children
205	      process.kill(-proc.pid, "SIGKILL");
206	    } catch { /* already dead */ }
207	  }
208	}
209	
210	interface ExecuteOptions {
211	  language: Language;
212	  code: string;
213	  timeout?: number;
214	  /** Keep process running after timeout instead of killing it. */
215	  background?: boolean;
216	  /**
217	   * Issue #45 — per-call cwd override for the shell language. When set,
218	   * the shell script runs in this directory instead of `#projectRoot`.
219	   * Non-shell languages keep their tmpDir sandbox cwd regardless (the
220	   * script file lives there). Used by Codex MCP handlers to pin shell
221	   * commands to a resolved project root when the spawning host inherited
222	   * a non-project cwd (e.g. $HOME).
223	   */
224	  cwd?: string;
225	}
226	
227	interface ExecuteFileOptions extends ExecuteOptions {
228	  path: string;
229	}
230	
231	export class PolyglotExecutor {
232	  #hardCapBytes: number;
233	  /**
234	   * Resolves the project root on every access. Stored as a thunk so the
235	   * executor stays in sync with server-side env-cascade resolvers (e.g.
236	   * `getProjectDir` in server.ts) instead of capturing a snapshot of
237	   * `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` at construction time. String inputs are wrapped
238	   * to preserve constructor backward compatibility.
239	   */
240	  #projectRootResolver: () => string;
241	  #runtimes: RuntimeMap;
242	
243	  /** PIDs of backgrounded processes — killed on cleanup to prevent zombies. */
244	  #backgroundedPids = new Set<number>();
245	
246	  constructor(opts?: {
247	    hardCapBytes?: number;
248	    projectRoot?: string | (() => string);
249	    runtimes?: RuntimeMap;
250	  }) {
251	    this.#hardCapBytes = opts?.hardCapBytes ?? 100 * 1024 * 1024; // 100MB
252	    const pr = opts?.projectRoot;
253	    if (typeof pr === "function") {
254	      this.#projectRootResolver = pr;
255	    } else if (typeof pr === "string") {
256	      this.#projectRootResolver = () => pr;
257	    } else {
258	      this.#projectRootResolver = () => process.cwd();
259	    }
260	    this.#runtimes = opts?.runtimes ?? detectRuntimes();
261	  }
262	
263	  get #projectRoot(): string {
264	    return this.#projectRootResolver();
265	  }
266	
267	  get runtimes(): RuntimeMap {
268	    return { ...this.#runtimes };
269	  }
270	
271	  /** Kill all backgrounded processes to prevent zombie/port-conflict issues. */
272	  cleanupBackgrounded(): void {
273	    for (const pid of this.#backgroundedPids) {
274	      try {
275	        // Kill process group on Unix to catch all children
276	        process.kill(isWin ? pid : -pid, "SIGTERM");
277	      } catch { /* already dead */ }
278	    }
279	    this.#backgroundedPids.clear();
280	  }
281	
282	  async execute(opts: ExecuteOptions): Promise<ExecResult> {
283	    const { language, code, timeout, background = false, cwd: cwdOverride } = opts;
284	    const tmpDir = mkdtempSync(join(OS_TMPDIR, ".ctx-mode-"));
285	
286	    try {
287	      const filePath = this.#writeScript(tmpDir, code, language);
288	      const cmd = buildCommand(this.#runtimes, language, filePath);
289	
290	      // Rust: compile then run
291	      if (cmd[0] === "__rust_compile_run__") {
292	        return await this.#compileAndRun(filePath, tmpDir, timeout);
293	      }
294	
295	      // Every language runs in the project directory so git, relative paths,
296	      // and other project-aware tools resolve naturally. The script FILE lives
297	      // in the sandbox tmpDir and is passed to the runtime by absolute path
298	      // (see buildCommand), so cwd is free to be the project root.
299	      //
300	      // Issue #788 — previously only `shell` used the project root; non-shell
301	      // runtimes (python/js/ts/…) used tmpDir, so repo-relative checks like
302	      // `pathlib.Path("package.json").exists()` silently failed depending on
303	      // the chosen language. Unifying cwd removes that surprise.
304	      // Issue #45 — `cwdOverride` lets per-call sites (Codex MCP handlers) pin
305	      // cwd without mutating process-wide state.
306	      const cwd = cwdOverride ?? this.#projectRoot;
307	      const result = await this.#spawn(cmd, cwd, tmpDir, timeout, background);
308	
309	      // Skip tmpDir cleanup if process was backgrounded — it may still need files
310	      if (!result.backgrounded) {
311	        cleanupTmpDir(tmpDir);
312	      }
313	
314	      return result;
315	    } catch (err) {
316	      cleanupTmpDir(tmpDir);
317	      throw err;
318	    }
319	  }
320	
321	  async executeFile(opts: ExecuteFileOptions): Promise<ExecResult> {
322	    const { path: filePath, language, code, timeout } = opts;
323	    const absolutePath = resolve(this.#projectRoot, filePath);
324	    const wrappedCode = this.#wrapWithFileContent(
325	      absolutePath,
326	      language,
327	      code,
328	    );
329	    return this.execute({ language, code: wrappedCode, timeout });
330	  }
331	
332	  #writeScript(tmpDir: string, code: string, language: Language): string {
333	    // Go needs a main package wrapper if not present
334	    if (language === "go" && !code.includes("package ")) {
335	      code = `package main\n\nimport "fmt"\n\nfunc main() {\n${code}\n}\n`;
336	    }
337	
338	    // PHP needs opening tag if not present
339	    if (language === "php" && !code.trimStart().startsWith("<?")) {
340	      code = `<?php\n${code}`;
341	    }
342	
343	    // Elixir: prepend compiled BEAM paths when inside a Mix project
344	    if (language === "elixir" && existsSync(join(this.#projectRoot, "mix.exs"))) {
345	      const escaped = JSON.stringify(join(this.#projectRoot, "_build/dev/lib"));
346	      code = `Path.wildcard(Path.join(${escaped}, "*/ebin"))\n|> Enum.each(&Code.prepend_path/1)\n\n${code}`;
347	    }
348	
349	    const fp = join(
350	      tmpDir,
351	      buildScriptFilename(
352	        language,
353	        process.platform,
354	        language === "shell" ? this.#runtimes.shell : null,
355	      ),
356	    );
357	    if (language === "shell") {
358	      const shellPath = this.#runtimes.shell;
359	      // #782 — on Windows Git Bash, rewrite bare `mvn` → `mvn.cmd` so Maven
360	      // uses its native Windows launcher (correct path handling) instead of
361	      // the broken mingw shell branch. No-op on non-Windows.
362	      const rewritten = rewriteWindowsBuildTools(code, process.platform);
363	      const shellCode = isWin && isPowerShell(shellPath)
364	        ? buildPowerShellScriptContent(rewritten)
365	        : rewritten;
366	      writeFileSync(
367	        fp,
368	        buildShellScriptContent(shellCode, process.env.PATH, process.platform),
369	        { encoding: "utf-8", mode: 0o700 },
370	      );
371	    } else {
372	      writeFileSync(fp, code, "utf-8");
373	    }
374	    return fp;
375	  }
376	
377	  async #compileAndRun(
378	    srcPath: string,
379	    cwd: string,
380	    timeout: number | undefined,
381	  ): Promise<ExecResult> {
382	    const binSuffix = isWin ? ".exe" : "";
383	    const binPath = srcPath.replace(/\.rs$/, "") + binSuffix;
384	
385	    // Compile — cap rustc invocation at 60s when caller didn't bound the
386	    // overall timeout (a hung compile shouldn't run forever even if the
387	    // caller is fine with a long-running binary afterwards).
388	    try {
389	      execFileSync("rustc", [srcPath, "-o", binPath], {
390	        cwd,
391	        timeout: timeout === undefined ? 60_000 : Math.min(timeout, 60_000),
392	        encoding: "utf-8",
393	        stdio: ["pipe", "pipe", "pipe"],
394	      });
395	    } catch (err: unknown) {
396	      const message = err instanceof Error ? (err as any).stderr || err.message : String(err);
397	      return {
398	        stdout: "",
399	        stderr: `Compilation failed:\n${message}`,
400	        exitCode: 1,
401	        timedOut: false,
402	      };
403	    }
404	
405	    // Run
406	    return this.#spawn([binPath], cwd, cwd, timeout);
407	  }
408	
409	  async #spawn(
410	    cmd: string[],
411	    cwd: string,
412	    sandboxTmpDir: string,
413	    timeout: number | undefined,
414	    background = false,
415	  ): Promise<ExecResult> {
416	    return new Promise((res) => {
417	      // Only .cmd/.bat shims need shell on Windows; real executables don't.
418	      // Using shell: true globally causes process-tree kill issues with MSYS2/Git Bash.
419	      // "bun" is included as defense-in-depth: bunCommand() prefers absolute
420	      // .exe paths now (#506), but if it falls back to the bare "bun" string
421	      // on Windows that resolution typically goes through a `bun.cmd` shim
422	      // (npm i -g bun) which CreateProcess can't execute without cmd.exe.
423	      const needsShell = isWin && ["tsx", "ts-node", "elixir", "bun", "dotnet-script"].includes(cmd[0]);
424	
425	      // On Windows with Git Bash, pass the script as `bash -c "source /posix/path"`
426	      // rather than `bash /path/to/script.sh`. This avoids MSYS2 path mangling
427	      // while still allowing MSYS_NO_PATHCONV to protect non-ASCII paths in commands.
428	      let spawnCmd = cmd[0];
429	      let spawnArgs: string[];
430	      if (isWin && cmd.length === 2 && cmd[1]) {
431	        const posixPath = cmd[1].replace(/\\/g, "/");
432	        spawnArgs = [posixPath];
433	      } else {
434	        spawnArgs = isWin
435	          ? cmd.slice(1).map(a => a.replace(/\\/g, "/"))
436	          : cmd.slice(1);
437	      }
438	
439	      // Common options shared by both spawn variants below.
440	      const commonOpts = {
441	        cwd,
442	        stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"] as ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
443	        env: this.#buildSafeEnv(sandboxTmpDir),
444	        // On Unix, create a new process group so killTree can kill all children
445	        detached: !isWin,
446	        // Hide the spawned-process console window on Windows. Without this,
447	        // child_process.spawn creates a visible window that intercepts stdout,
448	        // leaving the MCP response empty and popping a Git Bash terminal over
449	        // the user's IDE. Issue #384.
450	        ...buildSpawnOptions(process.platform),
451	      };
452	
453	      // DEP0190 fix: when shell is true (Windows .cmd/.bat shims), pass a
454	      // single command string instead of cmd + args array. Node.js warns
455	      // that args are unsafely concatenated when shell:true is combined with
456	      // the args-array form of spawn(). Colllapsing to a string avoids the
457	      // warning while preserving the same shell behavior.
458	      let proc: ReturnType<typeof spawn>;
459	      if (needsShell) {
460	        const fullCmd = [spawnCmd, ...spawnArgs]
461	          .map(a => /\s/.test(a) ? JSON.stringify(a) : a)
462	          .join(" ");
463	        proc = spawn(fullCmd, [], { ...commonOpts, shell: true });
464	      } else {
465	        proc = spawn(spawnCmd, spawnArgs, { ...commonOpts, shell: false });
466	      }
467	
468	      let timedOut = false;
469	      let resolved = false;
470	      // Issue #406 — if the caller didn't pass a timeout we don't fire one.
471	      // Timeout policy belongs to the MCP host/client (Claude Code, VSCode,
472	      // JetBrains all enforce their own RPC timeouts); imposing a second
473	      // policy here turned 30-minute Gradle/Maven/SBT builds into spurious
474	      // false negatives whenever the caller forgot the explicit value.
475	      const timer: NodeJS.Timeout | undefined = timeout === undefined ? undefined : setTimeout(() => {
476	        timedOut = true;
477	        if (background) {
478	          // Background mode: detach process, return partial output, keep running
479	          resolved = true;
480	          if (proc.pid) this.#backgroundedPids.add(proc.pid);
481	          proc.unref();
482	          // Do NOT destroy stdout/stderr — closing the read end of the pipe
483	          // sends SIGPIPE to the child on its next write, killing it.
484	          // Instead, replace the data listeners with no-op drains that
485	          // consume the stream without accumulating buffers. This keeps
486	          // the pipe open and prevents the child from blocking on a full
487	          // pipe buffer.
488	          if (proc.stdout) {
489	            proc.stdout.removeAllListeners("data");
490	            proc.stdout.on("data", () => {});
491	          }
492	          if (proc.stderr) {
493	            proc.stderr.removeAllListeners("data");
494	            proc.stderr.on("data", () => {});
495	          }
496	          const rawStdout = Buffer.concat(stdoutChunks).toString("utf-8");
497	          const rawStderr = Buffer.concat(stderrChunks).toString("utf-8");
498	          res({
499	            stdout: rawStdout,
500	            stderr: rawStderr,
501	            exitCode: 0,
502	            timedOut: true,
503	            backgrounded: true,
504	          });
505	        } else {
506	          killTree(proc);
507	        }
508	      }, timeout);
509	
510	      // Stream-level byte cap: kill the process once combined stdout+stderr
511	      // exceeds hardCapBytes. Without this, a command like `yes` or
512	      // `cat /dev/urandom | base64` can accumulate gigabytes in memory
513	      // before the timeout fires.
514	      const stdoutChunks: Buffer[] = [];
515	      const stderrChunks: Buffer[] = [];
516	      let totalBytes = 0;
517	      let capExceeded = false;
518	
519	      proc.stdout!.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => {
520	        totalBytes += chunk.length;
521	        if (totalBytes <= this.#hardCapBytes) {
522	          stdoutChunks.push(chunk);
523	        } else if (!capExceeded) {
524	          capExceeded = true;
525	          killTree(proc);
526	        }
527	      });
528	
529	      proc.stderr!.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => {
530	        totalBytes += chunk.length;
531	        if (totalBytes <= this.#hardCapBytes) {
532	          stderrChunks.push(chunk);
533	        } else if (!capExceeded) {
534	          capExceeded = true;
535	          killTree(proc);
536	        }
537	      });
538	
539	      proc.on("close", (exitCode) => {
540	        clearTimeout(timer);
541	        if (resolved) return; // Already resolved by background timeout
542	        const rawStdout = Buffer.concat(stdoutChunks).toString("utf-8");
543	        let rawStderr = Buffer.concat(stderrChunks).toString("utf-8");
544	
545	        if (capExceeded) {
546	          rawStderr += `\n[output capped at ${(this.#hardCapBytes / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(0)}MB — process killed]`;
547	        }
548	
549	        const stdout = rawStdout;
550	        const stderr = rawStderr;
551	
552	        res({
553	          stdout,
554	          stderr,
555	          exitCode: timedOut ? 1 : (exitCode ?? 1),
556	          timedOut,
557	        });
558	      });
559	
560	      proc.on("error", (err) => {
561	        clearTimeout(timer);
562	        if (resolved) return; // Already resolved by background timeout
563	        res({
564	          stdout: "",
565	          stderr: err.message,
566	          exitCode: 1,
567	          timedOut: false,
568	        });
569	      });
570	    });
571	  }
572	
573	  #buildSafeEnv(tmpDir: string): Record<string, string> {
574	    const realHome = process.env.HOME ?? process.env.USERPROFILE ?? tmpDir;
575	
576	    // Denylist: env vars that corrupt sandbox stdout, inject code, or break
577	    // language runtimes. Each entry is backed by CVE, MITRE, or live testing.
578	    // See: https://www.elttam.com/blog/env/, MITRE T1574.006
579	    const DENIED = new Set([
580	      // Shell — auto-execute scripts, override builtins
581	      "BASH_ENV",             // sourced by non-interactive bash
582	      "ENV",                  // sourced by sh/dash
583	      "PROMPT_COMMAND",       // runs before each prompt
584	      "PS4",                  // $(cmd) expansion in xtrace
585	      "SHELLOPTS",            // enables xtrace/verbose, dumps to stdout
586	      "BASHOPTS",             // bash-specific shell options
587	      "CDPATH",               // makes cd print to stdout
588	      "INPUTRC",              // readline key rebinding
589	      "BASH_XTRACEFD",        // redirects debug output to stdout
590	      // Node.js — require injection, inspector
591	      "NODE_OPTIONS",         // --require, --loader, --inspect
592	      "NODE_PATH",            // module search path injection
593	      // Python — stdlib override, startup injection
594	      "PYTHONSTARTUP",        // auto-executes in interactive mode
595	      "PYTHONHOME",           // overrides stdlib location (breaks Python)
596	      "PYTHONWARNINGS",       // triggers module import chain → RCE
597	      "PYTHONBREAKPOINT",     // arbitrary callable
598	      "PYTHONINSPECT",        // enters interactive mode after script
599	      // Ruby — option/module injection
600	      "RUBYOPT",              // injects CLI options (-r loads files)
601	      "RUBYLIB",              // module search path injection
602	      // Perl — option/module injection
603	      "PERL5OPT",             // injects CLI options (-M runs code)
604	      "PERL5LIB",             // module search path injection
605	      "PERLLIB",              // legacy module search path
606	      "PERL5DB",              // debugger command injection
607	      // Elixir/Erlang — eval injection
608	      "ERL_AFLAGS",           // prepends erl flags (-eval runs code)
609	      "ERL_FLAGS",            // appends erl flags
610	      "ELIXIR_ERL_OPTIONS",   // Elixir-specific erl flags
611	      "ERL_LIBS",             // beam file loading
612	      // Go — compiler/linker injection
613	      "GOFLAGS",              // injects go command flags
614	      "CGO_CFLAGS",           // C compiler flag injection
615	      "CGO_LDFLAGS",          // linker flag injection
616	      // Rust — compiler substitution
617	      "RUSTC",                // arbitrary compiler binary
618	      "RUSTC_WRAPPER",        // compiler wrapper injection
619	      "RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER",
620	      "CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC",
621	      "CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC_WRAPPER",
622	      "RUSTFLAGS",            // compiler flag injection
623	      // PHP — config injection
624	      "PHPRC",                // auto_prepend_file → RCE
625	      "PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR",     // additional .ini loading
626	      // R — startup script injection
627	      "R_PROFILE",            // site-wide R profile
628	      "R_PROFILE_USER",       // user R profile
629	      "R_HOME",               // R installation override
630	      // .NET / C# — runtime/startup hooks, additional deps
631	      "DOTNET_STARTUP_HOOKS",       // injects managed assemblies on startup
632	      "DOTNET_ADDITIONAL_DEPS",     // additional .deps.json injection
633	      "DOTNET_SHARED_STORE",        // shared assembly probe path injection
634	      "DOTNET_ROOT",                // arbitrary .NET runtime override
635	      "DOTNET_ROOT(x86)",           // 32-bit override
636	      "DOTNET_HOST_PATH",           // host binary substitution
637	      // .NET / C# — profiler attach (loads arbitrary DLL into dotnet host)
638	      // and IPC-based debugger/IL injection. PR #546 follow-up.
639	      // learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/runtime-config/debugging-profiling
640	      "CORECLR_PROFILER",                 // CLSID of profiler to attach
641	      "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH",            // path to profiler DLL
642	      "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_32",         // 32-bit specific profiler DLL
643	      "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_64",         // 64-bit specific profiler DLL
644	      "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_ARM32",      // ARM32 specific profiler DLL
645	      "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_ARM64",      // ARM64 specific profiler DLL
646	      "CORECLR_ENABLE_PROFILING",         // gates profiler load
647	      "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH",             // cross-platform alias
648	      "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_32",
649	      "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_64",
650	      "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_ARM32",
651	      "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_ARM64",
652	      "DOTNET_DiagnosticPorts",           // peer attach via diagnostic IPC
653	      "DOTNET_BUNDLE_EXTRACT_BASE_DIR",   // single-file extraction hijack
654	      // Dynamic linker — shared library injection
655	      "LD_PRELOAD",           // loads .so before all others (Linux)
656	      "DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES", // macOS equivalent of LD_PRELOAD
657	      // OpenSSL — engine loading
658	      "OPENSSL_CONF",         // loads engine modules → .so exec
659	      "OPENSSL_ENGINES",      // engine directory override
660	      // Compiler — binary substitution
661	      "CC",                   // C compiler override
662	      "CXX",                  // C++ compiler override
663	      "AR",                   // archiver override
664	      // Git — command injection via hooks/config
665	      "GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR",     // hook injection on git init
666	      "GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL",    // core.pager/editor runs commands
667	      "GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM",    // system-level config injection
668	      "GIT_EXEC_PATH",        // substitute git subcommands
669	      "GIT_SSH",              // arbitrary command instead of ssh
670	      "GIT_SSH_COMMAND",      // arbitrary ssh command
671	      "GIT_ASKPASS",          // arbitrary credential command
672	    ]);
673	
674	    // Start with parent env, then strip dangerous vars and apply overrides.
675	    // The `COMPlus_` prefix sweep covers every COMPlus_* synonym of the
676	    // DOTNET_* runtime knobs (.NET back-compat alias — case-insensitive).
677	    // PR #546 follow-up: closes the alias bypass for the explicit denylist
678	    // entries above.
679	    const env: Record<string, string> = {};
680	    for (const [key, val] of Object.entries(process.env)) {
681	      if (
682	        val !== undefined &&
683	        !DENIED.has(key) &&
684	        !key.startsWith("BASH_FUNC_") &&
685	        !/^COMPlus_/i.test(key)
686	      ) {
687	        env[key] = val;
688	      }
689	    }
690	
691	    // Sandbox overrides — forced values for correct sandbox behavior
692	    env["TMPDIR"] = tmpDir;
693	    env["HOME"] = realHome;
694	    env["LANG"] = "en_US.UTF-8";
695	    env["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1";
696	    env["PYTHONUNBUFFERED"] = "1";
697	    env["PYTHONUTF8"] = "1";
698	    env["NO_COLOR"] = "1";
699	    // Windows uses "Path" (not "PATH") — normalize to "PATH" for consistency
700	    if (isWin && !env["PATH"] && env["Path"]) {
701	      env["PATH"] = env["Path"];
702	      delete env["Path"];
703	    }
704	    if (!env["PATH"]) {
705	      env["PATH"] = isWin ? "" : "/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin";
706	    }
707	
708	    // Windows-critical PATH fixes.
709	    if (isWin) {
710	      // Do not carry global MSYS path-conversion blockers into Git Bash.
711	      // Native Windows tools launched from bash (notably git.exe) need MSYS
712	      // to convert /tmp-style arguments to Windows paths so sibling tools see
713	      // the same filesystem location (#791).
714	      for (const key of Object.keys(env)) {
715	        const upper = key.toUpperCase();
716	        if (upper === "MSYS_NO_PATHCONV" || upper === "MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL") {
717	          delete env[key];
718	        }
719	      }
720	
721	      const gitUsrBin = "C:\\Program Files\\Git\\usr\\bin";
722	      const gitBin = "C:\\Program Files\\Git\\bin";
723	      if (!env["PATH"].includes(gitUsrBin)) {
724	        env["PATH"] = `${gitUsrBin};${gitBin};${env["PATH"]}`;
725	      }
726	    }
727	
728	    // Ensure SSL_CERT_FILE is set so Python/Ruby HTTPS works in sandbox.
729	    if (!env["SSL_CERT_FILE"]) {
730	      const certPaths = isWin ? [] : [
731	        "/etc/ssl/cert.pem",                         // macOS, some Linux
732	        "/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt",         // Debian/Ubuntu/Alpine
733	        "/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt",           // RHEL/CentOS/Fedora
734	        "/etc/pki/ca-trust/extracted/pem/tls-ca-bundle.pem", // Fedora alt
735	      ];
736	      for (const p of certPaths) {
737	        if (existsSync(p)) {
738	          env["SSL_CERT_FILE"] = p;
739	          break;
740	        }
741	      }
742	    }
743	
744	    return env;
745	  }

747	  #wrapWithFileContent(
748	    absolutePath: string,
749	    language: Language,
750	    code: string,
751	  ): string {
752	    const escaped = JSON.stringify(absolutePath);
753	    switch (language) {
754	      case "javascript":
755	      case "typescript":
756	        return `const FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped};\nconst file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nconst FILE_CONTENT = require("fs").readFileSync(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, "utf-8");\n${code}`;
757	      case "python":
758	        return `FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped}\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nwith open(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as _f:\n    FILE_CONTENT = _f.read()\n${code}`;
759	      case "shell": {
760	        // Single-quote the path to prevent $, backtick, and ! expansion
761	        const sq = "'" + absolutePath.replace(/'/g, "'\\''") + "'";
762	        return `FILE_CONTENT_PATH=${sq}\nfile_path=${sq}\nFILE_CONTENT=$(cat ${sq})\n${code}`;
763	      }
764	      case "ruby":
765	        return `FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped}\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT = File.read(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, encoding: "utf-8")\n${code}`;
766	      case "go":
767	        return `package main\n\nimport (\n\t"fmt"\n\t"os"\n)\n\nvar FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped}\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\n\nfunc main() {\n\tb, _ := os.ReadFile(FILE_CONTENT_PATH)\n\tFILE_CONTENT := string(b)\n\t_ = FILE_CONTENT\n\t_ = fmt.Sprint()\n${code}\n}\n`;
768	      case "rust":
769	        return `#![allow(unused_variables)]\nuse std::fs;\n\nfn main() {\n    let file_content_path = ${escaped};\n    let file_path = file_content_path;\n    let file_content = fs::read_to_string(file_content_path).unwrap();\n${code}\n}\n`;
770	      case "php":
771	        return `<?php\n$FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped};\n$file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\n$FILE_CONTENT = file_get_contents($FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\n${code}`;
772	      case "perl":
773	        return `my $FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped};\nmy $file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nopen(my $fh, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', $FILE_CONTENT_PATH) or die "Cannot open: $!";\nmy $FILE_CONTENT = do { local $/; <$fh> };\nclose($fh);\n${code}`;
774	      case "r":
775	        return `FILE_CONTENT_PATH <- ${escaped}\nfile_path <- FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT <- readLines(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, warn=FALSE, encoding="UTF-8")\nFILE_CONTENT <- paste(FILE_CONTENT, collapse="\\n")\n${code}`;
776	      case "elixir":
777	        return `file_content_path = ${escaped}\nfile_path = file_content_path\nfile_content = File.read!(file_content_path)\n${code}`;
778	      case "csharp":
779	        // .csx forbids `using` directives after any other top-level statement
780	        // (CS1529). User code inside executeFile must use fully-qualified type
781	        // names (e.g. `System.Text.Json.JsonDocument`) instead of `using`.
782	        return `var FILE_CONTENT_PATH = ${escaped};\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nvar FILE_CONTENT = System.IO.File.ReadAllText(FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\n${code}`;
783	    }
784	  }

10	export type PermissionDecision = "allow" | "deny" | "ask";

13	  allow: string[];

18	// ==============================================================================
19	// Pattern Parsing

21	
22	/**
23	 * Extract the glob from a Bash permission pattern.
24	 * "Bash(sudo *)" returns "sudo *", "Read(.env)" returns null.
25	 */
26	export function parseBashPattern(pattern: string): string | null {
27	  // .+ is greedy: for "Bash(echo (foo))" it captures "echo (foo)"
28	  // because $ forces the final \) to match only the last paren.
29	  const match = pattern.match(/^Bash\((.+)\)$/);
30	  return match ? match[1] : null;
31	}
32	
33	/**
34	 * Parse any tool permission pattern like "ToolName(glob)".
35	 * Returns { tool, glob } or null if not a valid pattern.
36	 */
37	export function parseToolPattern(
38	  pattern: string,
39	): { tool: string; glob: string } | null {
40	  // .+ is greedy: for "Read(some(path))" it captures "some(path)"
41	  // because $ forces the final \) to match only the last paren.
42	  const match = pattern.match(/^(\w+)\((.+)\)$/);
43	  return match ? { tool: match[1], glob: match[2] } : null;
44	}
45	
46	// ==============================================================================
47	// Glob-to-Regex Conversion
48	// ==============================================================================
49	
50	/** Escape all regex special characters (including *). */
51	function escapeRegex(str: string): string {
52	  return str.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\\/\-]/g, "\\$&");
53	}
54	
55	/** Escape regex specials except *, then convert * to .* */
56	function convertGlobPart(glob: string): string {
57	  return glob
58	    .replace(/[.+?^${}()|[\]\\\/\-]/g, "\\$&")
59	    .replace(/\*/g, ".*");
60	}
61	
62	/**
63	 * Convert a Bash permission glob to a regex.
64	 *
65	 * Two formats:
66	 * - Colon: "tree:*" becomes /^tree(\s.*)?$/ (command with optional args)
67	 * - Space: "sudo *" becomes /^sudo .*$/  (literal glob match)
68	 */
69	export function globToRegex(
70	  glob: string,
71	  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,
72	): RegExp {
73	  let regexStr: string;
74	
75	  const colonIdx = glob.indexOf(":");
76	  if (colonIdx !== -1) {
77	    // Colon format: "command:argsGlob"
78	    const command = glob.slice(0, colonIdx);
79	    const argsGlob = glob.slice(colonIdx + 1);
80	    const escapedCmd = escapeRegex(command);
81	    const argsRegex = convertGlobPart(argsGlob);
82	    // Match command alone OR command + space + args
83	    regexStr = `^${escapedCmd}(\\s${argsRegex})?$`;
84	  } else {
85	    // Plain glob: "sudo *", "ls*", "* commit *"
86	    regexStr = `^${convertGlobPart(glob)}$`;
87	  }
88	
89	  return new RegExp(regexStr, caseInsensitive ? "i" : "");
90	}
91	
92	/**
93	 * Convert a file path glob to a regex.
94	 *
95	 * Unlike `globToRegex` (which handles command patterns with colon and
96	 * space semantics), this handles file path globs where:
97	 * - `**` matches any number of path segments (including zero)
98	 * - `*` matches anything except path separators
99	 * - Paths are matched with forward slashes (callers normalize first)
100	 */
101	export function fileGlobToRegex(
102	  glob: string,
103	  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,
104	): RegExp {
105	  let regexStr = "";
106	  let i = 0;
107	
108	  while (i < glob.length) {
109	    // Handle ** (globstar): match any number of directory segments
110	    if (glob[i] === "*" && glob[i + 1] === "*") {
111	      // **/ at the start or after a slash means "zero or more directories"
112	      if (i + 2 < glob.length && glob[i + 2] === "/") {
113	        regexStr += "(.*/)?";
114	        i += 3; // skip "*" "*" "/"
115	      } else {
116	        // Trailing ** matches everything
117	        regexStr += ".*";
118	        i += 2;
119	      }
120	    } else if (glob[i] === "*") {
121	      // Single * matches anything except /
122	      regexStr += "[^/]*";
123	      i++;
124	    } else if (glob[i] === "?") {
125	      regexStr += "[^/]";
126	      i++;
127	    } else {
128	      // Escape regex-special characters
129	      regexStr += glob[i].replace(/[.+^${}()|[\]\\\/\-]/g, "\\$&");
130	      i++;
131	    }
132	  }
133	
134	  return new RegExp(`^${regexStr}$`, caseInsensitive ? "i" : "");
135	}
136	
137	/**
138	 * Check if a command matches any Bash pattern in the list.
139	 * Returns the matching pattern string, or null.
140	 */
141	export function matchesAnyPattern(
142	  command: string,
143	  patterns: string[],
144	  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,
145	): string | null {
146	  for (const pattern of patterns) {
147	    const glob = parseBashPattern(pattern);
148	    if (!glob) continue;
149	    if (globToRegex(glob, caseInsensitive).test(command)) return pattern;
150	  }
151	  return null;
152	}

154	// ==============================================================================
155	// Chained Command Splitting & Subshell Extraction
156	// ==============================================================================
157	
158	function isEscaped(command: string, index: number): boolean {
159	  let backslashes = 0;
160	  for (let i = index - 1; i >= 0 && command[i] === "\\"; i--) {
161	    backslashes++;
162	  }
163	  return backslashes % 2 === 1;
164	}
165	
166	/**
167	 * Split a shell command on chain operators (&&, ||, ;, |, \n, \r, &) while
168	 * respecting single/double quotes, backticks, subshells, and escape backslashes.
169	 *
170	 * "echo hello && sudo rm -rf /" → ["echo hello", "sudo rm -rf /"]
171	 *
172	 * This prevents bypassing deny patterns by prepending innocent commands.
173	 */
174	export function splitChainedCommands(command: string): string[] {
175	  const parts: string[] = [];
176	  let current = "";
177	  let inSingle = false;
178	  let inDouble = false;
179	  let inBacktick = false;
180	  let dollarParenDepth = 0;
181	
182	  for (let i = 0; i < command.length; i++) {
183	    const ch = command[i];
184	    const escaped = isEscaped(command, i);
185	
186	    if (ch === "'" && !inDouble && !inBacktick && !escaped) {
187	      inSingle = !inSingle;
188	      current += ch;
189	    } else if (ch === '"' && !inSingle && !inBacktick && !escaped) {
190	      inDouble = !inDouble;
191	      current += ch;
192	    } else if (ch === "`" && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped) {
193	      inBacktick = !inBacktick;
194	      current += ch;
195	    } else if (!inSingle && !inDouble && !inBacktick) {
196	      if (ch === "$" && command[i + 1] === "(" && !escaped) {
197	        dollarParenDepth++;
198	        current += ch + command[i + 1];
199	        i++;
200	      } else if (dollarParenDepth > 0 && ch === "(" && !escaped) {
201	        dollarParenDepth++;
202	        current += ch;
203	      } else if (ch === ")" && dollarParenDepth > 0 && !escaped) {
204	        dollarParenDepth--;
205	        current += ch;
206	      } else if (
207	        dollarParenDepth === 0 &&
208	        (ch === ";" || ch === "\n" || ch === "\r") &&
209	        !escaped
210	      ) {
211	        parts.push(current.trim());
212	        current = "";
213	      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === "|" && command[i + 1] === "|") {
214	        parts.push(current.trim());
215	        current = "";
216	        i++; // skip second |
217	      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === "&" && command[i + 1] === "&") {
218	        parts.push(current.trim());
219	        current = "";
220	        i++; // skip second &
221	      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === "&" && !escaped) {
222	        parts.push(current.trim());
223	        current = "";
224	      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === "|") {
225	        // Single pipe — left side is a command too
226	        parts.push(current.trim());
227	        current = "";
228	      } else {
229	        current += ch;
230	      }
231	    } else {
232	      current += ch;
233	    }
234	  }
235	  if (current.trim()) parts.push(current.trim());
236	  return parts.filter((p) => p.length > 0);
237	}
238	
239	/**
240	 * Recursively extract all nested subshell commands from `$()` and `` `...` ``.
241	 * Handles escaping and quote contexts to ensure correct command boundary detection.
242	 */
243	export function extractSubshellCommands(command: string): string[] {
244	  const subshells: string[] = [];
245	  let inSingle = false;
246	  let inDouble = false;
247	  let backtickStart = -1;
248	
249	  const dollarParenStarts: number[] = [];
250	  const dollarParenDepths: number[] = [];
251	  let parenDepth = 0;
252	
253	  for (let i = 0; i < command.length; i++) {
254	    const ch = command[i];
255	    const escaped = isEscaped(command, i);
256	
257	    if (ch === "'" && !inDouble && backtickStart === -1 && !escaped) {
258	      inSingle = !inSingle;
259	    } else if (ch === '"' && !inSingle && backtickStart === -1 && !escaped) {
260	      inDouble = !inDouble;
261	    } else if (ch === "`" && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped) {
262	      if (backtickStart === -1) {
263	        backtickStart = i + 1;
264	      } else {
265	        const sub = command.slice(backtickStart, i);
266	        subshells.push(sub);
267	        subshells.push(...extractSubshellCommands(sub));
268	        backtickStart = -1;
269	      }
270	    } else if (!inSingle && backtickStart === -1) {
271	      if (ch === "$" && command[i + 1] === "(" && !escaped) {
272	        if (command[i + 2] === "(") {
273	          // Arithmetic expansion is not command execution, but nested command
274	          // substitutions inside it still get discovered by the scanner.
275	          parenDepth += 2;
276	          i += 2; // skip '(('
277	        } else {
278	          dollarParenStarts.push(i + 2);
279	          dollarParenDepths.push(parenDepth);
280	          parenDepth++;
281	          i++; // skip '('
282	        }
283	      } else if (ch === "(" && !escaped) {
284	        parenDepth++;
285	      } else if (ch === ")" && !escaped) {
286	        if (parenDepth > 0) {
287	          parenDepth--;
288	        }
289	        if (
290	          dollarParenDepths.length > 0 &&
291	          parenDepth === dollarParenDepths[dollarParenDepths.length - 1]
292	        ) {
293	          dollarParenDepths.pop();
294	          const start = dollarParenStarts.pop()!;
295	          const sub = command.slice(start, i);
296	          subshells.push(sub);
297	        }
298	      }
299	    }
300	  }
301	  return subshells;
302	}
303	
304	function collectCommandElements(command: string): string[] {
305	  const elements: string[] = [];
306	  const segments = splitChainedCommands(command);
307	  for (const segment of segments) {
308	    elements.push(segment);
309	    for (const subshell of extractSubshellCommands(segment)) {
310	      elements.push(...collectCommandElements(subshell));
311	    }
312	  }
313	  return elements;
314	}
