316	// ==============================================================================
317	// Settings Reader
318	// ==============================================================================
319	
320	/** Read one settings file and return a SecurityPolicy with only Bash patterns. */
321	function readSingleSettings(path: string): SecurityPolicy | null {
322	  let raw: string;
323	  try {
324	    raw = readFileSync(path, "utf-8");
325	  } catch {
326	    return null;
327	  }
328	
329	  let parsed: any;
330	  try {
331	    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
332	  } catch {
333	    return null;
334	  }
335	
336	  const perms = parsed?.permissions;
337	  if (!perms || typeof perms !== "object") return null;
338	
339	  const filterBash = (arr: unknown): string[] => {
340	    if (!Array.isArray(arr)) return [];
341	    return arr.filter(
342	      (p): p is string => typeof p === "string" && parseBashPattern(p) !== null,
343	    );
344	  };
345	
346	  return {
347	    allow: filterBash(perms.allow),
348	    deny: filterBash(perms.deny),
349	    ask: filterBash(perms.ask),
350	  };
351	}
352	
353	/**
354	 * Read Bash permission policies from up to 3 settings files.
355	 *
356	 * Returns policies in precedence order (most local first):
357	 *   1. .claude/settings.local.json  (project-local)
358	 *   2. .claude/settings.json        (project-shared)
359	 *   3. ~/.claude/settings.json      (global)
360	 *
361	 * Missing or invalid files are silently skipped.
362	 */
363	export function readBashPolicies(
364	  projectDir?: string,
365	  globalSettingsPath?: string,
366	): SecurityPolicy[] {
367	  const policies: SecurityPolicy[] = [];
368	
369	  if (projectDir) {
370	    const localPath = resolve(projectDir, ".claude", "settings.local.json");
371	    const localPolicy = readSingleSettings(localPath);
372	    if (localPolicy) policies.push(localPolicy);
373	
374	    const sharedPath = resolve(projectDir, ".claude", "settings.json");
375	    const sharedPolicy = readSingleSettings(sharedPath);
376	    if (sharedPolicy) policies.push(sharedPolicy);
377	  }
378	
379	  // Issue #451 round-3: read settings from EVERY adapter-specific global path
380	  // PLUS the claude global (defense in depth). When the caller passes an
381	  // explicit globalSettingsPath we honor it verbatim (back-compat with tests
382	  // and callers that already know which file to read).
383	  const globalPaths =
384	    globalSettingsPath !== undefined
385	      ? [globalSettingsPath]
386	      : resolveAdapterGlobalSettingsPaths();
387	
388	  for (const globalPath of globalPaths) {
389	    const globalPolicy = readSingleSettings(globalPath);
390	    if (globalPolicy) policies.push(globalPolicy);
391	  }
392	
393	  return policies;
394	}
395	
396	/**
397	 * Read deny patterns for a specific tool from settings files.
398	 *
399	 * Reads the same 3-tier settings as `readBashPolicies`, but extracts
400	 * only deny globs for the given tool. Used for Read and Grep enforcement
401	 * — checks if file paths should be blocked by deny patterns.
402	 *
403	 * Returns an array of arrays (one per settings file, in precedence order).
404	 * Each inner array contains the extracted glob strings.
405	 */
406	export function readToolDenyPatterns(
407	  toolName: string,
408	  projectDir?: string,
409	  globalSettingsPath?: string,
410	): string[][] {
411	  return readToolPermissionPatterns(toolName, "deny", projectDir, globalSettingsPath);
412	}
413	
414	/**
415	 * Read `permissions.{deny|allow}` globs for a tool from every settings file in
416	 * precedence order (project local → project shared → adapter globals).
417	 *
418	 * Generalizes the original deny-only reader so the project-boundary guard
419	 * (#852) can consult the SAME `permissions.allow` rules the user already
420	 * maintains for the host's `Read` tool — instead of inventing a context-mode-
421	 * specific opt-out env that would rot into dead code. A user who legitimately
422	 * needs an out-of-project read expresses it once, in the host config, e.g.
423	 * `"permissions": { "allow": ["Read(/var/log/**)"] }`, and both the host and
424	 * context-mode honor it.
425	 */
426	export function readToolPermissionPatterns(
427	  toolName: string,
428	  kind: "deny" | "allow",
429	  projectDir?: string,
430	  globalSettingsPath?: string,
431	): string[][] {
432	  const result: string[][] = [];
433	
434	  const extractGlobs = (path: string): string[] | null => {
435	    let raw: string;
436	    try {
437	      raw = readFileSync(path, "utf-8");
438	    } catch {
439	      return null;
440	    }
441	
442	    let parsed: any;
443	    try {
444	      parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
445	    } catch {
446	      return null;
447	    }
448	
449	    const entries = parsed?.permissions?.[kind];
450	    if (!Array.isArray(entries)) return [];
451	
452	    const globs: string[] = [];
453	    for (const entry of entries) {
454	      if (typeof entry !== "string") continue;
455	      const tp = parseToolPattern(entry);
456	      if (tp && tp.tool === toolName) {
457	        globs.push(tp.glob);
458	      }
459	    }
460	    return globs;
461	  };
462	
463	  if (projectDir) {
464	    const localGlobs = extractGlobs(
465	      resolve(projectDir, ".claude", "settings.local.json"),
466	    );
467	    if (localGlobs !== null) result.push(localGlobs);
468	
469	    const sharedGlobs = extractGlobs(
470	      resolve(projectDir, ".claude", "settings.json"),
471	    );
472	    if (sharedGlobs !== null) result.push(sharedGlobs);
473	  }
474	
475	  // Issue #451 round-3: union over every adapter-specific global path PLUS
476	  // claude global. Each settings file contributes its own globs array entry
477	  // so the precedence ordering downstream remains per-file rather than
478	  // collapsed.
479	  const globalPaths =
480	    globalSettingsPath !== undefined
481	      ? [globalSettingsPath]
482	      : resolveAdapterGlobalSettingsPaths();
483	
484	  for (const globalPath of globalPaths) {
485	    const globalGlobs = extractGlobs(globalPath);
486	    if (globalGlobs !== null) result.push(globalGlobs);
487	  }
488	
489	  return result;
490	}
491	
492	// ==============================================================================
493	// Decision Engine
494	// ==============================================================================
495	
496	interface CommandDecision {
497	  decision: PermissionDecision;
498	  matchedPattern?: string;
499	}
500	
501	/**
502	 * Evaluate a command against policies in precedence order.
503	 *
504	 * Splits chained commands (&&, ||, ;, |) and checks each segment
505	 * against deny patterns — prevents bypassing deny by prepending
506	 * innocent commands like "echo ok && sudo rm -rf /".
507	 *
508	 * Within each policy: deny > ask > allow (most restrictive wins).
509	 * First definitive match across policies wins.
510	 * Default (no match in any policy): "ask".
511	 */
512	export function evaluateCommand(
513	  command: string,
514	  policies: SecurityPolicy[],
515	  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === "win32" || process.platform === "darwin",
516	): CommandDecision {
517	  // Extract all main segments and nested subshell commands
518	  const allCommands = collectCommandElements(command);
519	
520	  // 1. Deny check: If ANY segment or subshell command is denied, block the entire command
521	  for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {
522	    for (const policy of policies) {
523	      const denyMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.deny, caseInsensitive);
524	      if (denyMatch) return { decision: "deny", matchedPattern: denyMatch };
525	    }
526	  }
527	
528	  // 2. Allow/Ask check: Evaluate segment-by-segment in precedence order.
529	  // The command is allowed if and only if EVERY segment and subshell is explicitly allowed.
530	  // If any element matches an ask pattern or matches no allow pattern, it defaults to ask.
531	  for (const policy of policies) {
532	    let allAllowed = true;
533	    let anyAsk = false;
534	    let matchedAskPattern: string | undefined;
535	    let matchedAllowPattern: string | undefined;
536	
537	    for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {
538	      const askMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.ask, caseInsensitive);
539	      if (askMatch) {
540	        anyAsk = true;
541	        matchedAskPattern = askMatch;
542	        break; // Ask wins immediately within this policy
543	      }
544	
545	      const allowMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.allow, caseInsensitive);
546	      if (!allowMatch) {
547	        allAllowed = false;
548	      } else {
549	        matchedAllowPattern = allowMatch;
550	      }
551	    }
552	
553	    if (anyAsk) {
554	      return { decision: "ask", matchedPattern: matchedAskPattern };
555	    }
556	
557	    if (allAllowed && allCommands.length > 0) {
558	      return { decision: "allow", matchedPattern: matchedAllowPattern };
559	    }
560	  }
561	
562	  return { decision: "ask" };
563	}
564	
565	/**
566	 * Server-side variant: only enforce deny patterns.
567	 *
568	 * The server has no UI for "ask" prompts, so allow/ask patterns are
569	 * irrelevant. Returns "deny" if any deny pattern matches, otherwise "allow".
570	 *
571	 * Also splits chained commands and nested subshells to prevent bypass.
572	 */
573	export function evaluateCommandDenyOnly(
574	  command: string,
575	  policies: SecurityPolicy[],
576	  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === "win32" || process.platform === "darwin",
577	): { decision: "deny" | "allow"; matchedPattern?: string } {
578	  const allCommands = collectCommandElements(command);
579	
580	  for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {
581	    for (const policy of policies) {
582	      const denyMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.deny, caseInsensitive);
583	      if (denyMatch) return { decision: "deny", matchedPattern: denyMatch };
584	    }
585	  }
586	
587	  return { decision: "allow" };
588	}
589	
590	// ==============================================================================
591	// File Path Evaluation
592	// ==============================================================================
593	
594	/**
595	 * Check if a file path should be denied based on deny globs.
596	 *
597	 * Normalizes backslashes to forward slashes before matching so that
598	 * Windows paths work with Unix-style glob patterns.
599	 *
600	 * When `projectRoot` is supplied, the path is also matched in its
601	 * fully-resolved absolute form **and** — when the file exists — in
602	 * its canonical form (`fs.realpathSync`). This prevents two classes
603	 * of bypass:
604	 *
605	 *   1. `..` traversal: a relative path like `../../.ssh/id_rsa` no
606	 *      longer evades absolute-path deny rules.
607	 *   2. Symlink escape: a project-local path whose realpath points
608	 *      outside the project (e.g. `safe.log -> ~/.ssh/id_rsa`) no
609	 *      longer evades absolute-path deny rules.
610	 *
611	 * realpath is best-effort: if the file does not exist yet (ENOENT)
612	 * or the syscall fails for any reason, the lexical resolved form is
613	 * still checked. This keeps the function usable for paths that will
614	 * be created during execution.
615	 */
616	export function evaluateFilePath(
617	  filePath: string,
618	  denyGlobs: string[][],
619	  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === "win32" || process.platform === "darwin",
620	  projectRoot?: string,
621	): { denied: boolean; matchedPattern?: string } {
622	  const toForward = (path: string): string => path.replace(/\\/g, "/");
623	
624	  // Match against the raw input, the lexically-resolved absolute path,
625	  // and the canonical (symlink-resolved) path when the file exists.
626	  // Deduplicated so absolute inputs and paths that don't cross symlinks
627	  // don't pay the matching cost multiple times.
628	  const candidates = new Set<string>();
629	  candidates.add(toForward(filePath));
630	  if (projectRoot) {
631	    const lexical = resolve(projectRoot, filePath);
632	    candidates.add(toForward(lexical));
633	    try {
634	      candidates.add(toForward(realpathSync(lexical)));
635	    } catch {
636	      // File does not exist yet, or realpath failed — rely on lexical form.
637	    }
638	  }
639	
640	  for (const globs of denyGlobs) {
641	    for (const glob of globs) {
642	      // Normalize the glob's path separators the same way candidates were
643	      // normalized — otherwise a Windows absolute deny rule like
644	      // `Read(C:\Users\...\secret.env)` parses with literal backslashes that
645	      // never match a forward-slash candidate.
646	      const regex = fileGlobToRegex(toForward(glob), caseInsensitive);
647	      for (const candidate of candidates) {
648	        if (regex.test(candidate)) {
649	          return { denied: true, matchedPattern: glob };
650	        }
651	      }
652	    }
653	  }
654	
655	  return { denied: false };
656	}
657	
658	// ==============================================================================
659	// Project-Boundary Containment (Issue #852)
660	// ==============================================================================
661	
662	/**
663	 * Pure, algorithmic (no-regex) test: does `filePath` resolve to a location
664	 * inside `projectRoot`?
665	 *
666	 * Issue #852 — `ctx_execute_file` previously fed its `path` argument straight
667	 * into `resolve(projectRoot, path)`. Because `path.resolve` lets an *absolute*
668	 * argument win outright, an agent could read any file on the host
669	 * (`/home/user/secret`, `/etc/passwd`) regardless of the project root, and
670	 * `../` traversal escaped just as easily. Claude Code's harness sandbox cannot
671	 * inspect MCP input params, so the user approving the MCP call could not see
672	 * that the path escaped the workspace. This guard re-anchors the path to the
673	 * project boundary.
674	 *
675	 * Containment is decided on the *resolved* form. When the file (or its parent
676	 * chain) exists, the symlink-canonical form is ALSO required to stay inside —
677	 * this closes the symlink-escape class (a project-local `safe.log` whose
678	 * realpath points at `~/.ssh/id_rsa`), mirroring `evaluateFilePath`.
679	 *
680	 * A path equal to the project root itself counts as inside. Comparison is
681	 * case-insensitive on Windows/macOS to match those filesystems' semantics.
682	 *
683	 * Returns `true` when `projectRoot` is falsy (no boundary to enforce) so the
684	 * caller's fail-open posture is preserved when the root cannot be resolved.
685	 */
686	export function isPathInsideProject(
687	  filePath: string,
688	  projectRoot: string | undefined,
689	  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === "win32" || process.platform === "darwin",
690	): boolean {
691	  if (!projectRoot) return true;
692	
693	  const root = resolve(projectRoot);
694	  const lexical = resolve(projectRoot, filePath);
695	
696	  const within = (root: string, candidate: string): boolean => {
697	    let a = root;
698	    let b = candidate;
699	    if (caseInsensitive) {
700	      a = a.toLowerCase();
701	      b = b.toLowerCase();
702	    }
703	    if (a === b) return true;
704	    // `path.relative` is pure string arithmetic — no regex. A candidate inside
705	    // the root yields a relative path that neither starts with `..` (escapes
706	    // upward) nor is absolute (a different drive/root on Windows that cannot be
707	    // expressed relatively).
708	    const rel = relative(a, b);
709	    if (rel === "") return true;
710	    if (rel === ".." || rel.startsWith(".." + sep)) return false;
711	    if (isAbsoluteRel(rel)) return false;
712	    return true;
713	  };
714	
715	  // Lexical containment is the primary gate.
716	  if (!within(root, lexical)) return false;
717	
718	  // Defense-in-depth: when the path (or a parent) is a symlink that points
719	  // outside the project, the canonical form must ALSO stay inside. Best-effort
720	  // — a not-yet-created file (ENOENT) falls back to the lexical decision above.
721	  try {
722	    const canonicalRoot = realpathSync(root);
723	    const canonical = realpathSync(lexical);
724	    if (!within(canonicalRoot, canonical)) return false;
725	  } catch {
726	    /* file does not exist yet / realpath failed — lexical decision stands */
727	  }
728	
729	  return true;
730	}
731	
732	/** Pure helper: is a `path.relative` result an absolute path? (no regex) */
733	function isAbsoluteRel(rel: string): boolean {
734	  if (rel.startsWith("/")) return true; // POSIX absolute
735	  // Windows drive-absolute: "C:\..." or "C:/..."
736	  if (rel.length >= 3 && rel[1] === ":" && (rel[2] === "\\" || rel[2] === "/")) {
737	    const c = rel.charCodeAt(0);
738	    return (c >= 65 && c <= 90) || (c >= 97 && c <= 122);
739	  }
740	  return false;
741	}
742	
743	/**
744	 * Decide whether `filePath` may be processed, given the project boundary AND
745	 * the user's existing host `Read(...)` allow rules.
746	 *
747	 * Decision order:
748	 *   1. Inside the project root  → allowed (the common case; no config needed).
749	 *   2. Outside the project, but matching a `permissions.allow` `Read(...)` glob
750	 *      the user already configured for the host → allowed. This is the
751	 *      principled escape hatch: a deliberate out-of-project read is expressed
752	 *      ONCE in the host config the user already maintains, reusing the same
753	 *      mechanism Claude Code itself uses to whitelist a path outside the
754	 *      sandbox — no context-mode-specific opt-out env that would rot into
755	 *      dead code.
756	 *   3. Outside the project, no allow match → denied (closes the #852 escape).
757	 *
758	 * `allowGlobs` has the same per-settings-file shape as the deny globs returned
759	 * by `readToolPermissionPatterns(toolName, "allow", …)`. Allow-matching reuses
760	 * `evaluateFilePath` so absolute/`..`/symlink-canonical candidate resolution is
761	 * identical to the deny path — one matcher, no divergence.
762	 *
763	 * Fail-open on an unknown project root (boundary cannot be computed) so the
764	 * guard never blocks legitimate in-project work when resolution fails.
765	 */
766	export function evaluateProjectContainment(
767	  filePath: string,
768	  projectRoot: string | undefined,
769	  allowGlobs: string[][] = [],
770	  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === "win32" || process.platform === "darwin",
771	): { allowed: boolean; reason: "inside" | "allow-rule" | "outside" } {
772	  if (isPathInsideProject(filePath, projectRoot, caseInsensitive)) {
773	    return { allowed: true, reason: "inside" };
774	  }
775	  // Outside the project — permit only if the user explicitly allowed this path
776	  // for the host Read tool. `evaluateFilePath` returns `denied:true` when a glob
777	  // MATCHES, so a match here means "explicitly allowed".
778	  if (allowGlobs.some((g) => g.length > 0)) {
779	    const matched = evaluateFilePath(filePath, allowGlobs, caseInsensitive, projectRoot);
780	    if (matched.denied) return { allowed: true, reason: "allow-rule" };
781	  }
782	  return { allowed: false, reason: "outside" };
783	}
784	
785	// ==============================================================================
786	// Shell-Escape Scanner
787	// ==============================================================================
788	
789	// Regex patterns that detect shell-escape calls in non-shell languages.
790	// Each pattern uses capture groups so that the embedded command string
791	// can be extracted from the last non-quote group.
792	//
793	// NOTE: These regexes contain literal strings like "execSync" — they are
794	// patterns for *detecting* shell escapes in user code, not actual usage.
795	const SHELL_ESCAPE_PATTERNS: Record<string, RegExp[]> = {
796	  python: [
797	    /os\.system\(\s*(['"])(.*?)\1\s*\)/g,
798	    /subprocess\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\(\s*(['"])(.*?)\1/g,
799	  ],
800	  javascript: [
801	    /exec(?:Sync|File|FileSync)?\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
802	    /spawn(?:Sync)?\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
803	  ],
804	  typescript: [
805	    /exec(?:Sync|File|FileSync)?\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
806	    /spawn(?:Sync)?\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
807	  ],
808	  ruby: [
809	    /system\(\s*(['"])(.*?)\1/g,
810	    /`(.*?)`/g,
811	  ],
812	  go: [
813	    /exec\.Command\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
814	  ],
815	  php: [
816	    /shell_exec\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
817	    /(?:^|[^.])exec\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
818	    /(?:^|[^.])system\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
819	    /passthru\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
820	    /proc_open\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
821	  ],
822	  rust: [
823	    /Command::new\(\s*(['"`])(.*?)\1/g,
824	  ],
825	};
826	
827	/**
828	 * Extract all string elements from a Python subprocess list call.
829	 *
830	 * subprocess.run(["rm", "-rf", "/"]) → "rm -rf /"
831	 *
832	 * This catches the list-of-strings form that the single-string regex misses.
833	 */
834	function extractPythonSubprocessListArgs(code: string): string[] {
835	  const commands: string[] = [];
836	  const pattern =
837	    /subprocess\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\(\s*\[([^\]]+)\]/g;
838	
839	  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;
840	  while ((match = pattern.exec(code)) !== null) {
841	    const listContent = match[1];
842	    const args = [...listContent.matchAll(/(['"])(.*?)\1/g)].map((m) => m[2]);
843	    if (args.length > 0) {
844	      commands.push(args.join(" "));
845	    }
846	  }
847	
848	  return commands;
849	}
850	
851	/**
852	 * Scan non-shell code for shell-escape calls and extract the embedded
853	 * command strings.
854	 *
855	 * Returns an array of command strings found in the code. For unknown
856	 * languages or code without shell-escape calls, returns an empty array.
857	 */
858	export function extractShellCommands(
859	  code: string,
860	  language: string,
861	): string[] {
862	  const patterns = SHELL_ESCAPE_PATTERNS[language];
863	  if (!patterns && language !== "python") return [];
864	
865	  const commands: string[] = [];
866	
867	  if (patterns) {
868	    for (const pattern of patterns) {
869	      // Reset lastIndex since we reuse the global regex
870	      pattern.lastIndex = 0;
871	
872	      let match: RegExpExecArray | null;
873	      while ((match = pattern.exec(code)) !== null) {
874	        // The command string is in the last capture group that isn't the
875	        // quote delimiter. For patterns with 2 groups (quote + content),
876	        // it's group 2. For Ruby backticks with 1 group, it's group 1.
877	        const command = match[match.length - 1];
878	        if (command) commands.push(command);
879	      }
880	    }
881	  }
882	
883	  // Python: also extract subprocess list-form args
884	  if (language === "python") {
885	    commands.push(...extractPythonSubprocessListArgs(code));
886	  }
887	
888	  return commands;
889	}
890

(Tools: *)\n- claude-code-guide: Use this agent when the user asks questions (\"Can Claude...\", \"Does Claude...\", \"How do I...\") about: (1) Claude Code (the CLI tool) - features, hooks, slash commands, MCP servers, settings, IDE integrations, keyboard shortcuts; (2) Claude Agent SDK - building custom agents; (3) Claude API (formerly Anthropic API) - Messages API for directly passing messages to Claude, Tool Runner (`client.beta.messages.tool_runner`) for running an agentic loop over your own tools, manual tool-use loops, Managed Agents for server-hosted agents with a managed sandbox, prompt caching, and general Anthropic SDK usage; (4) Claude Tag (Claude in Slack) - what it is, setting it up for a Slack workspace, `/install-slack-app`. **IMPORTANT:** Before spawning a new agent, check if there is already a running or recently completed claude-code-guide agent that you can continue via SendMessage. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. 'tokenhub.tencentmaas.com',\n44\t    port: parseInt(process.env.PROXY_TARGET_PORT || '443', 10),\n45\t  },\n46\t  localPort: parseInt(process.env.PROXY_PORT || '8080', 10),\n47\t  logDir: ==================== 初始化 ====================\n53\t\n54\tfs.mkdirSync(CONFIG.logDir, { recursive: true });\n55\tconst stamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, '-').replace('T', '_').replace('Z', '');\n56\tconst reqPath = path.join(CONFIG.logDir, `proxy-${stamp}-request.jsonl`);\n57\tconst respPath = path.join(CONFIG.logDir, `proxy-${stamp}-response.jsonl`);\n58\tconst summaryPath = path.join(CONFIG.logDir, `summary-${stamp}.jsonl`);\n59\t\n60\tlet requestCount = 0;\n61\tlet totalIn = 0;\n62\tlet totalOut = 0;\n63\tlet totalThinkingMs = 0;\n64\tlet totalOutputMs = 0;\n65\tlet totalTtfbMs = 0;\n66\tlet timingCount = 0;\n67\tconst toolCounts = {};\n68\tlet toolCallTotal = 0;\n69\t\n70\t// ==================== 工具函数 ====================\n71\t\n72\tfunction log(msg) {\n73\t  const t = new Date().toISOString().slice(11, 23); // HH:MM:SS.mmm\n74\t  console.log(`[${t}] ${msg}`);\n75\t}\n76\t\n77\tfunction tryJSON(str) {\n78\t  try { return JSON.parse(str); } catch { usage，message_delta 带最终 output_tokens\n85\t  for (const evt of sseEvents) {\n86\t    if (evt.type === 'message_start' && evt.data?.message?.usage) {\n87\t      usage = { ...evt.data.message.usage };\n88\t    } else if (evt.type === 'message_delta' && evt.data?.usage) {\n89\t      usage = { ...usage, ...evt.data.usage };\n90\t    }\n91\t  }\n92\t  // 非流式 JSON 响应\n93\t  if (!usage) {\n94\t    const json = typeof body === 'string' ? tryJSON(body) : body;\n95\t    const u = json?.usage;\n96\t    if (u) {\n97\t      if (u.input_tokens !== undefined) usage = u;                            // Anthropic\n98\t      else if (u.prompt_tokens !== undefined) {                              // OpenAI\n99\t        usage = {\n100\t          input_tokens: u.prompt_tokens,\n101\t          output_tokens: u.completion_tokens,\n102\t          total_tokens: u.total_tokens,\n103\t        };\n104\t      }\n105\t    }\n106\t  }\n107\t  return usage;\n108\t}\n109\t\n110\t/** 解析 SSE 事件流 → [{type, data, t_ms}, ...]，t_ms = 事件完整到达时距请求发出的毫秒数 */\n111\tfunction parseSSETimed(records) {\n112\t  const events = [];\n113\t  let carry = '';\n114\t  const pushBlock = (block, t) => {\n115\t    if (!block.trim()) return;\n116\t    const evt = { t_ms: t };\n117\t    for (const line of block.split('\\n')) {\n118\t      if (line.startsWith('event:')) {\n119\t        evt.type = line.slice(6).trim();\n120\t      } else if (line.startsWith('data:')) {\n121\t        const s = line.slice(5).trim();\n122\t        evt.data = tryJSON(s) || s;\n123\t      }\n124\t    }\n125\t    if (evt.type || evt.data !== undefined) events.push(evt);\n126\t  };\n127\t  for (const rec of records) {\n128\t    carry += rec.b.toString('utf-8');\n129\t    const parts = carry.split('\\n\\n');\n130\t    carry = parts.pop(); // 最后一段可能不完整，留到下一 chunk\n131\t    for (const block of parts) pushBlock(block, rec.t);\n132\t  }\n133\t  const lastT = records.length ? records[records.length - 1].t : 0;\n134\t  pushBlock(carry, lastT);\n135\t  carry = '';\n136\t  return events;\n137\t}\n138\t\n139\t/** 从带时间戳的 SSE 事件计算耗时分解 */\n140\tfunction computeTiming(events) {\n141\t  if (!events.length) return null;\n142\t  const ttfb_ms = events[0].t_ms;\n143\t  let thinkingStart = null, outputStart = null, endMs = events[events.length - 1].t_ms;\n144\t  for (const e of events) {\n145\t    const cb = e.data?.content_block;\n146\t    if (e.type === 'content_block_start') {\n147\t      if ((cb?.type === 'thinking' || cb?.type === 'redacted_thinking') && thinkingStart === null) thinkingStart = e.t_ms;\n148\t      if ((cb?.type === 'text' || cb?.type === 'tool_use') && outputStart === null) outputStart = e.t_ms;\n149\t    } else if (e.type === 'content_block_delta') {\n150\t      const dt = e.data?.delta?.type;\n151\t      if (dt === 'thinking_delta' && thinkingStart === null) thinkingStart = e.t_ms;\n152\t      if ((dt === 'text_delta' || dt === 'input_json_delta') && outputStart === null) outputStart = e.t_ms;\n153\t    } else if (e.type === 'message_stop') {\n154\t      endMs = e.t_ms;\n155\t    }\n156\t  }\n157\t  // thinking 结束 = 第一个输出块开始（text 或 tool_use）\n158\t  const thinkingEnd = thinkingStart !== null && outputStart !== null ? outputStart : null;\n159\t  return {\n160\t    ttfb_ms,                                              // 首字节到达（上游排队+模型启动）\n161\t    thinking_ms: thinkingStart !== null && thinkingEnd !== null ? thinkingEnd - thinkingStart : 0, // 思维链耗时\n162\t    output_ms: outputStart !== null ? endMs - outputStart : 0,    // 正文/工具调用生成耗时\n163\t    first_token_ms: (thinkingStart ?? outputStart ?? endMs) - ttfb_ms, // 首个内容 token 相对首字节\n164\t    total_ms: endMs,\n165\t  };\n166\t}\n167\t\n168\t/** 从响应中提取本轮模型调用的工具名列表 */\n169\tfunction extractToolCalls(sseEvents, body) {\n170\t  const tools = [];\n171\t  // 流式：content_block_start 事件携带 tool_use 块\n172\t  for (const evt of sseEvents) {\n173\t    const cb = evt.data?.content_block;\n174\t    if (evt.type === 'content_block_start' && cb?.type === 'tool_use') {\n175\t      tools.push(cb.name + (cb.id ? `(${cb.id.slice(-6)})` : ''));\n176\t    }\n177\t  }\n178\t  // 非流式：content 数组里的 tool_use 块\n179\t  if (!tools.length) {\n180\t    const json = typeof body === 'string' ? tryJSON(body) : body;\n181\t    for (const cb of json?.content || []) {\n182\t      if (cb?.type === 'tool_use') tools.push(cb.name + (cb.id ? `(${cb.id.slice(-6)})` : ''));\n183\t    }\n184\t  }\n185\t  return tools;\n186\t}\n187\t\n188\t/** 请求消息摘要：每条消息的 role / 内容形态 / 长度（不展开全文） */\n189\tfunction summarizeMessages(messages) {\n190\t  if (!Array.isArray(messages)) return null;\n191\t  return messages.map(m => {\n192\t    const c = m.content;\n193\t    if (typeof c === 'string') return { role: m.role, form: 'string', chars: c.length };\n194\t    if (Array.isArray(c)) {\n195\t      return { role: m.role, form: 'blocks', blocks: c.map(b => b.type || '?') };\n196\t    }\n197\t    return { role: m.role, form: typeof c };\n198\t  });\n199\t}\n200\t\n201\t// ==================== 代理服务器 ====================\n202\t\n203\tconst server = http.createServer((clientReq, );\n357\t\n358\t    proxyReq.on('error', err => {\n359\t      log(`#${reqId} !! 上游错误: ${err.message}`);\n360\t      if (!clientRes.headersSent) {\n361\t        clientRes.writeHead(502, { ${name.padEnd(16)} ${String(n).padStart(3)} 次`);\n442\t    }\n443\t  }\n444\t  console.log('  ------------------------------------------------------');\n445\t  console.log(`  请求流日志    :           "content": "PreToolUse:Read hook additional context: <context_guidance>\n  <tip>\n    Reading to Edit the file? Read is correct — Edit needs the exact bytes in your conversation to match against.\n    Reading to analyze, summarize, or extract from the file? Use mcp__plugin_context-mode_context-mode__ctx_execute_file(path, language, code) — the bytes stay in the sandbox and only what your code prints enters your conversation.\n  </tip>\n</context_guidance>\n\nContents of E:\\WT\\save-tokens\\context-mode\\CLAUDE.md:\n\n# context-mode — MANDATORY routing rules\n\ncontext-mode MCP tools available. Rules protect context window from flooding. One unrouted command dumps 56 KB into context.\n\n## Think in Code — MANDATORY\n\nAnalyze/count/filter/compare/search/parse/transform data: **write code** via `ctx_execute(language, code)`, `console.log()` only the answer. Do NOT read raw data into context. PROGRAM the analysis, not COMPUTE it. Pure JavaScript — Node.js built-ins only (`fs`, `path`, `child_process`). `try/catch`, handle `null`/`undefined`. One script replaces ten tool calls.\n\n## BLOCKED — do NOT attempt\n\n### curl / wget — BLOCKED\nIntercepted and replaced with error. Do NOT retry.\nUse: `ctx_fetch_and_index(url, source)` or `ctx_execute(language: \"javascript\", code: \"const r = await fetch(...)\")`\n\n### Inline HTTP — BLOCKED\n`fetch('http`, `requests.get(`, `requests.post(`, `http.get(`, `http.request(` — intercepted. Do NOT retry.\nUse: `ctx_execute(language, code)` — only stdout enters context\n\n### WebFetch — BLOCKED\nUse: `ctx_fetch_and_index(url, source)` then `ctx_search(queries)`\n\n## REDIRECTED — use sandbox\n\n### Bash (>20 lines output)\nBash ONLY for: `git`, `mkdir`, `rm`, `mv`, `cd`, `ls`, `npm install`, `pip install`.\nOtherwise: `ctx_batch_execute(commands, queries)` or `ctx_execute(language: \"shell\", code: \"...\")`\n\n### Read (for analysis)\nReading to **Edit** → Read correct. Reading to **analyze/explore/summarize** → `ctx_execute_file(path, language, code)`.\n\n### Grep — may flood context\nUse `ctx_execute(language: \"shell\", code: \"grep ...\")` in sandbox.\n\n## Tool selection\n\n0. **MEMORY**: `ctx_search(sort: \"timeline\")` — after resume, check prior context before asking user.\n1. **GATHER**: `ctx_batch_execute(commands, queries)` — runs all commands, auto-indexes, returns search. ONE call replaces 30+. Each command: `{label: \"header\", command: \"...\"}`.\n2. **FOLLOW-UP**: `ctx_search(queries: [\"q1\", \"q2\", ...])` — all questions as array, ONE call (default relevance mode).\n3. **PROCESSING**: `ctx_execute(language, code)` | `ctx_execute_file(path, language, code)` — sandbox, only stdout enters context.\n4. **WEB**: `ctx_fetch_and_index(url, source)` then `ctx_search(queries)` — raw HTML never enters context.\n5. **INDEX**: `ctx_index(content, source)` — store in FTS5 for later search.\n\n## Parallel I/O batches\n\nFor multi-URL fetches or multi-API calls, **always** include `concurrency: N` (1-8):\n\n- `ctx_batch_execute(commands: [3+ network commands], concurrency: 5)` — gh, curl, dig, docker inspect, multi-region cloud queries\n- `ctx_fetch_and_index(requests: [{url, source}, ...], concurrency: 5)` — multi-URL batch fetch\n\n**Use concurrency 4-8** for I/O-bound work (network calls, API queries). **Keep concurrency 1** for CPU-bound (npm test, build, lint) or commands sharing state (ports, lock files, same-repo writes).\n\nGitHub API rate-limit: cap at 4 for `gh` calls.\n\n## Subagent routing\n\nRouting block auto-injected into subagent prompts. Bash-type subagents upgraded to general-purpose. No manual instruction needed.\n\n## Output\n\nWrite artifacts to FILES — never inline. Return: file path + 1-line description.\nDescriptive source labels for `ctx_search(source: \"label\")`.\n\n## Session Continuity\n\nSkills, roles, and decisions persist for the entire session. Do not abandon them as the conversation grows.\n\n## Memory\n\nSession history is persistent and searchable. 批量，最干净的节省机制。\n20\t6. **FloodGuard**：60s 窗口，3 次 softCap，8 次 block。按 agent 分桶。\n21\t7. **Session Snapshot**：声称\"零截断零丢失\"，实际是有损摘要+搜索指针。maxBytes 被忽略。\n22\t\n23\t### 核心结论\n24\tToken 节省不是单一机制驱动，而是               "text": "---\n\n## 项目分析：`save-tokens`\n\n### 📌 一句话总结\n\n这是一个 **Claude Code token 消耗测量与优化实验项目**，核心目标是量化并验证 context-mode 插件对 token 的节省效果。\n\n---\n\n### 🧱 项目结构（三层架构）\n\n```\nsave-tokens/\n├── context-mode/          ← 第一层：被测试对象（第三方 MCP 插件）\n├── proxy.mjs              ← 第二层：测量工具（API 代理，全量拦截）\n├── analyze-proxy-log.js    ← 第三层：分析工具（token 对比报告）\n├── proxy-logs/            ← 产出：代理捕获的原始日志\n├── .TinnoveCode/          ← 产出：token 状态追踪\n├── .workbuddy/            ← 产出：工作日志/记忆\n└── .claude/               ← 配置：启用 context-mode 插件\n```\n\n---\n\n### 1. context-mode（被测试对象）\n\n- **来源**：GitHub [mksglu/context-mode](https://github.com/mksglu/context-mode)，v1.0.169，Elastic-2.0 协议\n- **核心功能**：通过 MCP 协议提供沙箱化代码执行、FTS5 全文搜索知识库、意图驱动搜索，声称能节省 98% 的上下文窗口\n- **支持平台**：Claude Code、Gemini CLI、VS Code Copilot、OpenCode、Codex CLI、Cursor、JetBrains Copilot、Kimi、Kiro、Qwen Code 等 15+ 平台\n- **源码规模**：核心 TypeScript 约 50 万字符（`server.ts` 225KB + `cli.ts` 89KB + `store.ts` 74KB + `executor.ts` 32KB + `security.ts` 30KB 等）\n- **7 个独立 token 节省机制**（来自工作日志分析）：\n  1. 沙箱执行（stdout 只返回摘要）\n  2. 输出外部化（>100KB 自动索引，5KB~100KB 之间是盲区）\n  3. FTS5 知识库（双索引 + RRF 融合 + proximity 重排序）\n  4. 字节安全截断\n  5. Fetch 预览（3KB/384B 限制）\n  6. FloodGuard（搜索节流）\n  7. Session Snapshot（有损摘要 + 搜索指针）\n\n---\n\n### 2. proxy.mjs（测量工具，自建）\n\n- **零依赖** HTTP 代理服务器，仅用 Node.js 内置模块\n- **监听** `localhost:8080`，**转发**到 `https://tokenhub.tencentmaas.com`（腾讯内部的 LLM API 网关）\n- **产出三类日志**：\n  | 文件 | 内容 | 写入时机 |\n  |------|------|----------|\n  | `proxy-*-request.jsonl` | 完整请求体、工具列表、消息摘要 | 请求到达即写 |\n  | `proxy-*-response.jsonl` | 完整响应、SSE 事件、token 用量、耗时分解 | 响应结束才写 |\n  | `summary-*.jsonl` | 模型/耗时/状态码/token/累计 | 每条响应实时追加 |\n\n- **特色能力**：\n  - SSE 事件逐条解析 + 时间戳，计算 **TTFB / 思维链耗时 / 生成耗时 / 首个 token 延迟**\n  - 兼容 Anthropic (`input_tokens`) 和 OpenAI (`prompt_tokens`) 两种 usage 格式\n  - 实时累计统计 + Ctrl+C 退出打印完整报告（工具调用排行榜）\n  - 真实日志验证了 `deepseek-v4-pro` 经 tokenhub 透出 thinking 块（think 值非 0）\n\n---\n\n### 3. analyze-proxy-log.js（分析工具，自建）\n\n- 三种运行模式：\n  1. **单会话摘要**：token 总量/均值、耗时、工具排行\n  2. **A/B 对比**（对照组 vs 实验组）：计算输入 token 节省率\n  3. **`--by-prompt`**：按用户提示词分组，看每个任务花多少 token\n\n---\n\n### 4. 当前状态与发现\n\n根据工作日志和代理日志：\n\n| 发现 | 详情 |\n|------|------|\n| **context-mode 插件未生效** | `.claude.json` 中 mcpServers 为空，`installed_plugins.json` 无 context-mode，代理日志中 `tools_available` 无 `mcp__*` 工具，请求体无 `<context_window_protection>` 注入标记 |\n| **模型使用** | `deepseek-v4-pro`，经腾讯 tokenhub 网关 |\n| **会话特征** | 29 个工具全为原生（无插件工具），请求体 100KB~188KB，输入 token 25K~43K/轮，缓存读取 4K~47K |\n| **安装方式** | `claude mcp add context-mode -- npx -y context-mode` 或本地 `node cli.bundle.mjs` |\n\n---\n\n### 5. 项目工作流\n\n```\n启动代理 → 设置 ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL → 正常使用 Claude Code\n                                        ↓\n                              所有 API 流量被代理拦截\n                                        ↓\n                              请求/响应实时落盘 JSONL\n                                        ↓\n                              Ctrl+C 退出 → 打印统计\n                                        ↓\n                     analyze-proxy-log.js 分析对比\n```\n\n---\n\n### 6. 总结\n\n这是一个 **实验基础设施项目**，而非产品代码。目标是通过代理全量捕获 API 流量，量化验证 context-mode 插件的 token 节省效果。目前基础设施已就绪（代理 + 分析工具完善），但 **context-mode 插件尚未在本机成功安装和生效**，因此 A/B 对比实验还未真正执行。"

              "content": "1\t/**\n2\t * lifecycle — Process lifecycle guard for MCP server.\n3\t *\n4\t * Detects parent process death (ppid polling) and OS signals to prevent\n5\t * orphaned MCP server processes consuming 100% CPU (issue #103).\n6\t *\n7\t * Stdin close is NOT used as a *standalone* shutdown signal — the MCP stdio\n8\t * transport owns stdin and transient pipe events cause spurious -32000\n9\t * errors (#236). We do, however, treat stdin EOF as a hint to re-run the\n10\t * parent-liveness probe immediately (instead of waiting up to 30 s for the\n11\t * next poll tick), which closes the multi-day CPU-spin window seen in\n12\t * #311/#388 without reintroducing the false-positive shutdowns of #236.\n13\t *\n14\t * Additionally, for MCP BRIDGE CHILDREN only (CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH>0), a\n15\t * request-idle self-shutdown reaps a child that a pi/omp sub-context abandoned\n16\t * while its long-lived parent keeps running (#854) — gated so the depth-0\n17\t * keep-alive servers #602 restored are never reaped, never via stdin EOF, and\n18\t * never while a tool call is in flight (#643).\n19\t *\n20\t * Cross-platform: macOS, Linux, Windows.\n21\t */\n22\t\n23\timport { execFileSync } from \"node:child_process\";\n24\t\n25\texport interface LifecycleGuardOptions {\n26\t  /** Interval in ms to check parent liveness. Default: 30_000 */\n27\t  checkIntervalMs?: number;\n28\t  /** Called when parent death or OS signal is detected. */\n29\t  onShutdown: () => void;\n30\t  /** Injectable parent-alive check (for testing). Default: ppid-based check. */\n31\t  isParentAlive?: () => boolean;\n32\t  /**\n33\t   * #854: request-idle shutdown timeout (ms) for MCP bridge children. Default:\n34\t   * {@link bridgeChildIdleTimeoutMs}() — 0 (disabled) unless CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH>0.\n35\t   * Exposed for testing.\n36\t   */\n37\t  bridgeIdleMs?: number;\n38\t}\n39\t\n40\t/** Read grandparent PID via `ps -o ppid= -p $PPID`. Returns NaN on failure or Windows. */\n41\tfunction readGrandparentPpidImpl(): number {\n42\t  if (process.platform === \"win32\") return NaN;\n43\t  const ppid = process.ppid;\n44\t  if (!ppid || ppid <= 1) return NaN;\n45\t  try {\n46\t    const out = execFileSync(\"ps\", [\"-o\", \"ppid=\", \"-p\", String(ppid)], {\n47\t      encoding: \"utf-8\",\n48\t      timeout: 2000,\n49\t      stdio: [\"ignore\", \"pipe\", \"ignore\"],\n50\t    }).trim();\n51\t    const n = parseInt(out, 10);\n52\t    return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : NaN;\n53\t  } catch {\n54\t    return NaN;\n55\t  }\n56\t}\n57\t\n58\t/** Injectable dependencies for {@link makeDefaultIsParentAlive}. */\n59\texport interface IsParentAliveDeps {\n60\t  /** Read the current ppid. Default: `() => process.ppid`. */\n61\t  getPpid?: () => number;\n62\t  /** Read the grandparent ppid. Default: ps-based POSIX probe, NaN on Windows. */\n63\t  readGrandparentPpid?: () => number;\n64\t}\n65\t\n66\t/**\n67\t * Build a parent-liveness check that handles the npm-exec wrapper case (#311).\n68\t *\n69\t * A plain ppid comparison misses Claude Code sessions launched via\n70\t * `start.mjs → npm exec → context-mode server`: when Claude Code dies,\n71\t * `start.mjs` reparents to init but `npm exec` stays alive, so the server's\n72\t * direct ppid never changes. We additionally check whether the grandparent\n73\t * process has been reparented to init (PID 1). When the original grandparent\n74\t * was already 1 (daemonized startup) the check is skipped, and on Windows\n75\t * where there's no cheap `ps` equivalent we also skip — so this change is\n76\t * strictly additive to the previous behavior.\n77\t *\n78\t * Exported for unit-testing with injected readers. Production code uses\n79\t * {@link defaultIsParentAlive} (captured once at module load).\n80\t */\n81\texport function makeDefaultIsParentAlive(deps: IsParentAliveDeps = {}): () => boolean {\n82\t  const getPpid = deps.getPpid ?? (() => process.ppid);\n83\t  const readGp = deps.readGrandparentPpid ?? readGrandparentPpidImpl;\n84\t  const originalPpid = getPpid();\n85\t  const originalGrandparentPpid = readGp();\n86\t\n87\t  return () => {\n88\t    const ppid = getPpid();\n89\t    if (ppid !== originalPpid) return false;\n90\t    if (ppid === 0 || ppid === 1) return false;\n91\t\n92\t    // Grandparent orphan check (#311): npm-exec wrappers stay alive past the\n93\t    // session owner. If our grandparent is now PID 1 but wasn't at startup,\n94\t    // the wrapping chain is orphaned and we should shut down.\n95\t    if (!Number.isNaN(originalGrandparentPpid) && originalGrandparentPpid > 1) {\n96\t      if (readGp() === 1) return false;\n97\t    }\n98\t\n99\t    return true;\n100\t  };\n101\t}\n102\t\n103\tconst defaultIsParentAlive = makeDefaultIsParentAlive();\n104\t\n105\t/**\n106\t * Resolve the parent-liveness poll interval based on context (#534).\n107\t *\n108\t * When this process is the MCP bridge child spawned by the Pi adapter\n109\t * (`bootstrapMCPTools` in `src/adapters/pi/mcp-bridge.ts` sets\n110\t * `CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH=1` in the child env), we tighten the poll to\n111\t * 1 s. The Pi parent can disappear in under 50 ms (`pi --help` prints\n112\t * usage and returns), so the default 30 s window leaves a long-lived\n113\t * CPU-spinning orphan. For top-level MCP servers (depth 0 / absent) we\n114\t * keep the original 30 s cadence — the existing #311/#388 ppid + stdin\n115\t * recovery paths already cover Claude Code style hosts.\n116\t *\n117\t * Exported for unit-testing.\n118\t */\n119\texport function lifecycleGuardIntervalForEnv(\n120\t  env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env,\n121\t): number {\n122\t  const raw = env.CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH;\n123\t  if (raw === undefined) return 30_000;\n124\t  const depth = Number.parseInt(raw, 10);\n125\t  if (!Number.isFinite(depth) || depth <= 0) return 30_000;\n126\t  return 1000;\n127\t}\n128\t\n129\t/**\n130\t * #854: idle-shutdown timeout (ms) for an MCP BRIDGE CHILD. Returns 0 (disabled)\n131\t * unless this process is a bridge child (CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH>0). depth-0 /\n132\t * absent always returns 0, so the long-lived keep-alive servers that #602\n133\t * restored are NEVER reaped on idle. Default for bridge children is 3 min;\n134\t * override with CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_IDLE_MS (a non-positive value disables it).\n135\t * The reaper additionally never fires while a tool call is in flight (see\n136\t * {@link noteRequestStart}), so the window only bounds how fast *abandoned*\n137\t * children drain — it does not cap legitimate long-running calls.\n138\t *\n139\t * Exported for unit-testing.\n140\t */\n141\texport function bridgeChildIdleTimeoutMs(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): number {\n142\t  const depth = Number.parseInt(env.CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_DEPTH ?? \"\", 10);\n143\t  if (!Number.isFinite(depth) || depth <= 0) return 0;\n144\t  const raw = env.CONTEXT_MODE_BRIDGE_IDLE_MS;\n145\t  if (raw !== undefined) {\n146\t    const v = Number.parseInt(raw, 10);\n147\t    return Number.isFinite(v) && v > 0 ? v : 0;\n148\t  }\n149\t  return 180_000;\n150\t}\n151\t\n152\t/**\n153\t * #854 / #868: human-readable notice emitted when an idle bridge child is\n154\t * released. DX-tuned — human units (seconds, not raw ms), reassures that the\n155\t * helper reconnects automatically (it respawns on the next ctx_* call, #583),\n156\t * and drops the alarming \"self-shutdown\" jargon. Pure + exported so the wording\n157\t * is pinned by a test and stays grep-friendly via the #854 tag. Note: after the\n158\t * #868 fix this fires ONLY for sub-context / non-interactive children — the\n159\t * foreground interactive session's child runs with the reaper disabled.\n160\t */\n161\texport function idleReapMessage(idleMs: number): string {\n162\t  const seconds = Math.round(idleMs / 1000);\n163\t  return `[context-mode] Released an idle MCP helper after ${seconds}s of inactivity to free memory; it reconnects automatically on next use. (#854)`;\n164\t}\n165\t\n166\t// #854 idle-reaper state, module-level by design: an MCP server is exactly one\n167\t// process (one StdioServerTransport + one lifecycle guard), so these are never\n168\t// shared across concurrent servers in production. Exported for testing.\n205\t */\n206\texport function attachMcpActivityTap(\n207\t  transport: { onmessage?: (message: unknown, extra?: unknown) => unknown } | null | undefined,\n208\t): void {\n209\t  if (!transport) return;\n210\t  const prev = typeof transport.onmessage === \"function\" ? transport.onmessage.bind(transport) : null;\n211\t  if (!prev) return;\n212\t  transport.onmessage = (message: unknown, extra?: unknown) => {\n213\t    try { noteMcpActivity(); } catch { /* never break message dispatch */ }\n214\t    return prev(message, extra);\n215\t  };\n216\t}\n217\t\n218\t/**\n219\t * Start the lifecycle guard.               "tool_use_id": "toolu_4111565ebc624c02b0b34d9b",
              "type": "tool_result",
              "content": "1\t/**\n2\t * truncate — Pure string truncation and escaping utilities for context-mode.\n3\t *\n4\t * These helpers are used by the core ContentStore (chunking) and\n5\t * SessionDB (snapshot building). They are extracted here so any\n6\t * consumer can import them without pulling in the full store or executor.\n7\t */\n8\t\n9\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n10\t// Internal: byte-safe prefix\n11\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n12\t\n13\t/**\n14\t * Return the longest character-prefix of `str` whose UTF-8 encoding is at\n15\t * most `maxBytes` bytes. Uses binary search to avoid O(n²) scanning. Returns\n16\t * \"\" when `maxBytes` is <= 0 so callers never exceed their budget.\n17\t *\n18\t * Guards against splitting a UTF-16 surrogate pair: if the prefix would end\n19\t * on a lone high surrogate, back off one code unit so the result round-trips\n20\t * through UTF-8 without producing a U+FFFD replacement character.\n21\t */\n22\tfunction byteSafePrefix(str: string, maxBytes: number): string {\n23\t  if (maxBytes <= 0) return \"\";\n24\t  if (Buffer.byteLength(str) <= maxBytes) return str;\n25\t\n26\t  let lo = 0;\n27\t  let hi = str.length;\n28\t  while (lo < hi) {\n29\t    const mid = (lo + hi + 1) >> 1;\n30\t    if (Buffer.byteLength(str.slice(0, mid)) <= maxBytes) {\n31\t      lo = mid;\n32\t    } else {\n33\t      hi = mid - 1;\n34\t    }\n35\t  }\n36\t\n37\t  // If we landed between a high and low surrogate, back off so the prefix\n38\t  // ends on a valid code point boundary.\n39\t  if (lo > 0) {\n40\t    const code = str.charCodeAt(lo - 1);\n41\t    if (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdbff) lo -= 1;\n42\t  }\n43\t  return str.slice(0, lo);\n44\t}\n45\t\n46\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n47\t// Char-count prefix (UTF-16 surrogate-safe)\n48\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n49\t\n50\t/**\n51\t * Return a prefix of `str` no longer than `maxChars` UTF-16 code units, with\n52\t * the cut backed off by one unit if it would split a surrogate pair. Mirrors\n53\t * the surrogate-safety semantics of the internal `byteSafePrefix`, but uses\n54\t * a character budget rather than a byte budget.\n55\t *\n56\t * Use this instead of bare `str.slice(0, n)` whenever the result is later\n57\t * JSON-encoded for an RFC 8259-strict consumer (e.g. tool_result payloads\n58\t * sent back to the host LLM API). `JSON.stringify` will emit a lone high\n59\t * surrogate as a literal `\\uD8xx` escape, which is invalid JSON.\n60\t *\n61\t * @param str      - Input string.\n62\t * @param maxChars - Hard cap in UTF-16 code units.\n63\t */\n64\texport function charSafePrefix(str: string, maxChars: number): string {\n65\t  if (maxChars <= 0) return \"\";\n66\t  if (str.length <= maxChars) return str;\n67\t\n68\t  let end = maxChars;\n69\t  const code = str.charCodeAt(end - 1);\n70\t  if (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdbff) end -= 1;\n71\t  return str.slice(0, end);\n72\t}\n73\t\n74\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n75\t// JSON truncation\n76\t// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n77\t\n78\t/**\n79\t * Serialize a value to JSON, then truncate the result to `maxBytes` bytes.\n80\t * If truncation occurs, the string is cut at a UTF-8-safe boundary and\n81\t * \"... [truncated]\" is appended. The result is NOT guaranteed to be valid\n82\t * JSON after truncation — it is suitable only for display/logging.\n83\t *\n84\t * The returned string is always <= `maxBytes` bytes. When `maxBytes` is\n85\t * smaller than the marker, the marker itself is byte-safely truncated.\n86\t *\n87\t * @param value    - Any JSON-serializable value.\n88\t * @param maxBytes - Maximum byte length of the returned string.\n89\t * @param indent   - JSON indentation spaces (default 2). Pass 0 for compact.\n90\t */\n91\texport function truncateJSON(\n92\t  value: unknown,\n93\t  maxBytes: number,\n94\t  indent: number = 2,\n95\t): string {\n96\t  const serialized = JSON.stringify(value, null, indent) ?? \"null\";\n97\t  if (Buffer.byteLength(serialized) <= maxBytes) return serialized;\n98\t\n99\t  const marker = \"... (err as any).stderr || err.message : String(err);\n397\t      return {\n398\t        stdout: \"\",\n399\t        stderr: `Compilation failed:\\n${message}`,\n400\t        exitCode: 1,\n401\t        timedOut: false,\n402\t      };\n403\t    }\n404\t\n405\t    // Run\n406\t    return this.#spawn([binPath], cwd, cwd, timeout);\n407\t  }\n408\t\n409\t  async #spawn(\n410\t    cmd: string[],\n411\t    cwd: string,\n412\t    sandboxTmpDir: string,\n413\t    timeout: number | undefined,\n414\t    background = false,\n415\t  ): Promise<ExecResult> {\n416\t    return new Promise((res) => {\n417\t      // Only .cmd/.bat shims need shell on Windows; real executables don't.\n418\t      // Using shell: true globally causes process-tree kill issues with MSYS2/Git Bash.\n419\t      // \"bun\" is included as defense-in-depth: bunCommand() prefers absolute\n420\t      // .exe paths now (#506), but if it falls back to the bare \"bun\" string\n421\t      // on Windows that resolution typically goes through a `bun.cmd` shim\n422\t      // (npm i -g bun) which CreateProcess can't execute without cmd.exe.\n423\t      const needsShell = isWin && [\"tsx\", \"ts-node\", \"elixir\", \"bun\", \"dotnet-script\"].includes(cmd[0]);\n424\t\n425\t      // On Windows with Git Bash, pass the script as `bash -c \"source /posix/path\"`\n426\t      // rather than `bash /path/to/script.sh`. This avoids MSYS2 path mangling\n427\t      // while still allowing MSYS_NO_PATHCONV to protect non-ASCII paths in commands.\n428\t      let spawnCmd = cmd[0];\n429\t      let spawnArgs: string[];\n430\t      if (isWin && cmd.length === 2 && cmd[1]) {\n431\t        const posixPath = cmd[1].replace(/\\\\/g, \"/\");\n432\t        spawnArgs = [posixPath];\n433\t      } else {\n434\t        spawnArgs = isWin\n435\t          ? cmd.slice(1).map(a => a.replace(/\\\\/g, \"/\"))\n436\t          : cmd.slice(1);\n437\t      }\n438\t\n439\t      // Common options shared by both spawn variants below.\n440\t      const commonOpts = {\n441\t        cwd,\n442\t        stdio: [\"ignore\", \"pipe\", \"pipe\"] as [\"ignore\", \"pipe\", \"pipe\"],\n443\t        env: this.#buildSafeEnv(sandboxTmpDir),\n444\t        // On Unix, create a new process group so killTree can kill all children\n445\t        detached: !isWin,\n446\t        // Hide the spawned-process console window on Windows. Without this,\n447\t        // child_process.spawn creates a visible window that intercepts stdout,\n448\t        // leaving the MCP response empty and popping a Git Bash terminal over\n449\t        // the user's IDE. Issue #384.\n450\t        ...buildSpawnOptions(process.platform),\n451\t      };\n452\t\n453\t      // DEP0190 fix: when shell is true (Windows .cmd/.bat shims), pass a\n454\t      // single command string instead of cmd + args array. Node.js warns\n455\t      // that args are unsafely concatenated when shell:true is combined with\n456\t      // the args-array form of spawn(). Colllapsing to a string avoids the\n457\t      // warning while preserving the same shell behavior.\n458\t      let proc: ReturnType<typeof spawn>;\n459\t      if (needsShell) {\n460\t        const fullCmd = [spawnCmd, ...spawnArgs]\n461\t          .map(a => /\\s/.test(a) ? JSON.stringify(a) : a)\n462\t          .join(\" \");\n463\t        proc = spawn(fullCmd, [], { ...commonOpts, shell: true });\n464\t      } else {\n465\t        proc = spawn(spawnCmd, spawnArgs, { ...commonOpts, shell: false });\n466\t      }\n467\t\n468\t      let timedOut = false;\n469\t      let resolved = false;\n470\t      // Issue #406 — if the caller didn't pass a timeout we don't fire one.\n471\t      // Timeout policy belongs to the MCP host/client (Claude Code, VSCode,\n472\t      // JetBrains all enforce their own RPC timeouts); imposing a second\n473\t      // policy here turned 30-minute Gradle/Maven/SBT builds into spurious\n474\t      // false negatives whenever the caller forgot the explicit value.\n475\t      const timer: NodeJS.Timeout | undefined = timeout === undefined ? undefined : setTimeout(() => {\n476\t        timedOut = true;\n477\t        if (background) {\n478\t          // Background mode: detach process, return partial output, keep running\n479\t          resolved = true;\n480\t          if (proc.pid) this.#backgroundedPids.add(proc.pid);\n481\t          proc.unref();\n482\t          // Do NOT destroy stdout/stderr — closing the read end of the pipe\n483\t          // sends SIGPIPE to the child on its next write, killing it.\n484\t          // Instead, replace the data listeners with no-op drains that\n485\t          // consume the stream without accumulating buffers. This keeps\n486\t          // the pipe open and prevents the child from blocking on a full\n487\t          // pipe buffer.\n488\t          if (proc.stdout) {\n489\t            proc.stdout.removeAllListeners(\"data\");\n490\t            proc.stdout.on(\"data\", () => {});\n491\t          }\n492\t          if (proc.stderr) {\n493\t            proc.stderr.removeAllListeners(\"data\");\n494\t            proc.stderr.on(\"data\", () => {});\n495\t          }\n496\t          const rawStdout = Buffer.concat(stdoutChunks).toString(\"utf-8\");\n497\t          const rawStderr = Buffer.concat(stderrChunks).toString(\"utf-8\");\n498\t          res({\n499\t            stdout: rawStdout,\n500\t            stderr: rawStderr,\n501\t            exitCode: 0,\n502\t            timedOut: true,\n503\t            backgrounded: true,\n504\t          });\n505\t        } else {\n506\t          killTree(proc);\n507\t        }\n508\t      }, timeout);\n509\t\n510\t      // Stream-level byte cap: kill the process once combined stdout+stderr\n511\t      // exceeds hardCapBytes. 1 : (exitCode ?? 1),\n556\t          timedOut,\n557\t        });\n558\t      });\n559\t\n560\t      proc.on(\"error\", (err) => {\n561\t        clearTimeout(timer);\n562\t        if (resolved) return; // Already resolved by background timeout\n563\t        res({\n564\t          stdout: \"\",\n565\t          stderr: err.message,\n566\t          exitCode: 1,\n567\t          timedOut: false,\n568\t        });\n569\t      });\n570\t    });\n571\t  }\n572\t\n573\t  #buildSafeEnv(tmpDir: string): Record<string, string> {\n574\t    const realHome = process.env.HOME ??
