(foo)\"\n28\t  // because $ forces the final \\) to match only the last paren.\n29\t  const match = pattern.match(/^Bash\\((.+)\\)$/);\n30\t  return match ? match[1] : null;\n31\t}\n32\t\n33\t/**\n34\t * Parse any tool permission pattern like \"ToolName(glob)\".\n35\t * Returns { tool, glob } or null if not a valid pattern.\n36\t */\n37\texport function parseToolPattern(\n38\t  pattern: string,\n39\t): { tool: string; glob: string } | null {\n40\t  // .+ is greedy: for \"Read(some(path))\" it captures \"some(path)\"\n41\t  // because $ forces the final \\) to match only the last paren.\n42\t  const match = pattern.match(/^(\\w+)\\((.+)\\)$/);\n43\t  return match ? { tool: match[1], glob: match[2] } : null;\n44\t}\n45\t\n46\t// ==============================================================================\n47\t// Glob-to-Regex Conversion\n48\t// ==============================================================================\n49\t\n50\t/** Escape all regex special characters (including *). */\n51\tfunction escapeRegex(str: string): string {\n52\t  return str.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\\\/\\-]/g, \"\\\\$&\");\n53\t}\n54\t\n55\t/** Escape regex specials except *, then convert * to .* */\n56\tfunction convertGlobPart(glob: string): string {\n57\t  return glob\n58\t    .replace(/[.+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\\\/\\-]/g, \"\\\\$&\")\n59\t    .replace(/\\*/g, \".*\");\n60\t}\n61\t\n62\t/**\n63\t * Convert a Bash permission glob to a regex.\n64\t *\n65\t * Two formats:\n66\t * - Colon: \"tree:*\" becomes /^tree(\\s.*)?$/ (command with optional args)\n67\t * - Space: \"sudo *\" becomes /^sudo .*$/  (literal glob match)\n68\t */\n69\texport function globToRegex(\n70\t  glob: string,\n71\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,\n72\t): RegExp {\n73\t  let regexStr: string;\n74\t\n75\t  const colonIdx = glob.indexOf(\":\");\n76\t  if (colonIdx !== -1) {\n77\t    // Colon format: \"command:argsGlob\"\n78\t    const command = glob.slice(0, colonIdx);\n79\t    const argsGlob = glob.slice(colonIdx + 1);\n80\t    const escapedCmd = escapeRegex(command);\n81\t    const argsRegex = convertGlobPart(argsGlob);\n82\t    // Match command alone OR command + space + args\n83\t    regexStr = `^${escapedCmd}(\\\\s${argsRegex})?$`;\n84\t  } else {\n85\t    // Plain glob: \"sudo *\", \"ls*\", \"* commit *\"\n86\t    regexStr = `^${convertGlobPart(glob)}$`;\n87\t  }\n88\t\n89\t  return new RegExp(regexStr, caseInsensitive ? \"i\" : \"\");\n90\t}\n91\t\n92\t/**\n93\t * Convert a file path glob to a regex.\n94\t *\n95\t * Unlike `globToRegex` (which handles command patterns with colon and\n96\t * space semantics), this handles file path globs where:\n97\t * - `**` matches any number of path segments (including zero)\n98\t * - `*` matches anything except path separators\n99\t * - Paths are matched with forward slashes (callers normalize first)\n100\t */\n101\texport function fileGlobToRegex(\n102\t  glob: string,\n103\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,\n104\t): RegExp {\n105\t  let regexStr = \"\";\n106\t  let i = 0;\n107\t\n108\t  while (i < glob.length) {\n109\t    // Handle ** (globstar): match any number of directory segments\n110\t    if (glob[i] === \"*\" && glob[i + 1] === \"*\") {\n111\t      // **/ at the start or after a slash means \"zero or more directories\"\n112\t      if (i + 2 < glob.length && glob[i + 2] === \"/\") {\n113\t        regexStr += \"(.*/)?\";\n114\t        i += 3; // skip \"*\" \"*\" \"/\"\n115\t      } else {\n116\t        // Trailing ** matches everything\n117\t        regexStr += \".*\";\n118\t        i += 2;\n119\t      }\n120\t    } else if (glob[i] === \"*\") {\n121\t      // Single * matches anything except /\n122\t      regexStr += \"[^/]*\";\n123\t      i++;\n124\t    } else if (glob[i] === \"?\") {\n125\t      regexStr += \"[^/]\";\n126\t      i++;\n127\t    } else {\n128\t      // Escape regex-special characters\n129\t      regexStr += glob[i].replace(/[.+^${}()|[\\]\\\\\\/\\-]/g, \"\\\\$&\");\n130\t      i++;\n131\t    }\n132\t  }\n133\t\n134\t  return new RegExp(`^${regexStr}$`, caseInsensitive ? \"i\" : \"\");\n135\t}\n136\t\n137\t/**\n138\t * Check if a command matches any Bash pattern in the list.\n139\t * Returns the matching pattern string, or null.\n140\t */\n141\texport function matchesAnyPattern(\n142\t  command: string,\n143\t  patterns: string[],\n144\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = false,\n145\t): string | null {\n146\t  for (const pattern of patterns) {\n147\t    const glob = parseBashPattern(pattern);\n148\t    if (!glob) continue;\n149\t    if (globToRegex(glob, caseInsensitive).test(command)) return pattern;\n150\t  }\n151\t  return null;\n152\t}\n153\t\n154\t// ==============================================================================\n155\t// Chained Command Splitting & Subshell Extraction\n156\t// ==============================================================================\n157\t\n158\tfunction isEscaped(command: string, index: number): boolean {\n159\t  let backslashes = 0;\n160\t  for (let i = index - 1; i >= 0 && command[i] === \"\\\\\"; i--) {\n161\t    backslashes++;\n162\t  }\n163\t  return backslashes % 2 === 1;\n164\t}\n165\t\n166\t/**\n167\t * Split a shell command on chain operators (&&, ||, ;, |, \\n, \\r, &) while\n168\t * respecting single/double quotes, backticks, subshells, and escape backslashes.\n169\t *\n170\t * \"echo hello && sudo rm -rf /\" → [\"echo hello\", \"sudo rm -rf /\"]\n171\t *\n172\t * This prevents bypassing deny patterns by prepending innocent commands.\n173\t */\n174\texport function splitChainedCommands(command: string): string[] {\n175\t  const parts: string[] = [];\n176\t  let current = \"\";\n177\t  let inSingle = false;\n178\t  let inDouble = false;\n179\t  let inBacktick = false;\n180\t  let dollarParenDepth = 0;\n181\t\n182\t  for (let i = 0; i < command.length; i++) {\n183\t    const ch = command[i];\n184\t    const escaped = isEscaped(command, i);\n185\t\n186\t    if (ch === \"'\" && !inDouble && !inBacktick && !escaped) {\n187\t      inSingle = !inSingle;\n188\t      current += ch;\n189\t    } else if (ch === '\"' && !inSingle && !inBacktick && !escaped) {\n190\t      inDouble = !inDouble;\n191\t      current += ch;\n192\t    } else if (ch === \"`\" && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped) {\n193\t      inBacktick = !inBacktick;\n194\t      current += ch;\n195\t    } else if (!inSingle && !inDouble && !inBacktick) {\n196\t      if (ch === \"$\" && command[i + 1] === \"(\" && !escaped) {\n197\t        dollarParenDepth++;\n198\t        current += ch + command[i + 1];\n199\t        i++;\n200\t      } else if (dollarParenDepth > 0 && ch === \"(\" && !escaped) {\n201\t        dollarParenDepth++;\n202\t        current += ch;\n203\t      } else if (ch === \")\" && dollarParenDepth > 0 && !escaped) {\n204\t        dollarParenDepth--;\n205\t        current += ch;\n206\t      } else if (\n207\t        dollarParenDepth === 0 &&\n208\t        (ch === \";\" || ch === \"\\n\" || ch === \"\\r\") &&\n209\t        !escaped\n210\t      ) {\n211\t        parts.push(current.trim());\n212\t        current = \"\";\n213\t      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === \"|\" && command[i + 1] === \"|\") {\n214\t        parts.push(current.trim());\n215\t        current = \"\";\n216\t        i++; // skip second |\n217\t      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === \"&\" && command[i + 1] === \"&\") {\n218\t        parts.push(current.trim());\n219\t        current = \"\";\n220\t        i++; // skip second &\n221\t      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === \"&\" && !escaped) {\n222\t        parts.push(current.trim());\n223\t        current = \"\";\n224\t      } else if (dollarParenDepth === 0 && ch === \"|\") {\n225\t        // Single pipe — left side is a command too\n226\t        parts.push(current.trim());\n227\t        current = \"\";\n228\t      } else {\n229\t        current += ch;\n230\t      }\n231\t    } else {\n232\t      current += ch;\n233\t    }\n234\t  }\n235\t  if (current.trim()) parts.push(current.trim());\n236\t  return parts.filter((p) => p.length > 0);\n237\t}\n238\t\n239\t/**\n240\t * Recursively extract all nested subshell commands from `$()` and `` `...` ``.\n241\t * Handles escaping and quote contexts to ensure correct command boundary detection.\n242\t */\n243\texport function extractSubshellCommands(command: string): string[] {\n244\t  const subshells: string[] = [];\n245\t  let inSingle = false;\n246\t  let inDouble = false;\n247\t  let backtickStart = -1;\n248\t\n249\t  const dollarParenStarts: number[] = [];\n250\t  const dollarParenDepths: number[] = [];\n251\t  let parenDepth = 0;\n252\t\n253\t  for (let i = 0; i < command.length; i++) {\n254\t    const ch = command[i];\n255\t    const escaped = isEscaped(command, i);\n256\t\n257\t    if (ch === \"'\" && !inDouble && backtickStart === -1 && !escaped) {\n258\t      inSingle = !inSingle;\n259\t    } else if (ch === '\"' && !inSingle && backtickStart === -1 && !escaped) {\n260\t      inDouble = !inDouble;\n261\t    } else if (ch === \"`\" && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped) {\n262\t      if (backtickStart === -1) {\n263\t        backtickStart = i + 1;\n264\t      } else {\n265\t        const sub = command.slice(backtickStart, i);\n266\t        subshells.push(sub);\n267\t        subshells.push(...extractSubshellCommands(sub));\n268\t        backtickStart = -1;\n269\t      }\n270\t    } else if (!inSingle && backtickStart === -1) {\n271\t      if (ch === \"$\" && command[i + 1] === \"(\" && !escaped) {\n272\t        if (command[i + 2] === \"(\") {\n273\t          // Arithmetic expansion is not command execution, but nested command\n274\t          // substitutions inside it still get discovered by the scanner.\n275\t          parenDepth += 2;\n276\t          i += 2; // skip '(('\n277\t        } else {\n278\t          dollarParenStarts.push(i + 2);\n279\t          dollarParenDepths.push(parenDepth);\n280\t          parenDepth++;\n281\t          i++; // skip '('\n282\t        }\n283\t      } else if (ch === \"(\" && !escaped) {\n284\t        parenDepth++;\n285\t      } else if (ch === \")\" && !escaped) {\n286\t        if (parenDepth > 0) {\n287\t          parenDepth--;\n288\t        }\n289\t        if (\n290\t          dollarParenDepths.length > 0 &&\n291\t          parenDepth === dollarParenDepths[dollarParenDepths.length - 1]\n292\t        ) {\n293\t          dollarParenDepths.pop();\n294\t          const start = dollarParenStarts.pop()!;\n295\t          const sub = command.slice(start, i);\n296\t          subshells.push(sub);\n297\t        }\n298\t      }\n299\t    }\n300\t  }\n301\t  return subshells;\n302\t}\n303\t\n304\tfunction collectCommandElements(command: string): string[] {\n305\t  const elements: string[] = [];\n306\t  const segments = splitChainedCommands(command);\n307\t  for (const segment of segments) {\n308\t    elements.push(segment);\n309\t    for (const subshell of extractSubshellCommands(segment)) {\n310\t      elements.push(...collectCommandElements(subshell));\n311\t    }\n312\t  }\n313\t  return elements;\n314\t}\n315\t\n316\t// ==============================================================================\n317\t// Settings Reader\n318\t// ==============================================================================\n319\t\n320\t/** Read one settings file and return a SecurityPolicy with only Bash patterns. */\n321\tfunction readSingleSettings(path: string): SecurityPolicy | null {\n322\t  let raw: string;\n323\t  try {\n324\t    raw = readFileSync(path, \"utf-8\");\n325\t  } catch {\n326\t    return null;\n327\t  }\n328\t\n329\t  let parsed: any;\n330\t  try {\n331\t    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);\n332\t  } catch {\n333\t    return null;\n334\t  }\n335\t\n336\t  const perms = parsed?.permissions;\n337\t  if (!perms || typeof perms !== \"object\") return null;\n338\t\n339\t  const filterBash = (arr: unknown): string[] => {\n340\t    if (!Array.isArray(arr)) return [];\n341\t    return arr.filter(\n342\t      (p): p is string => typeof p === \"string\" && parseBashPattern(p) !== null,\n343\t    );\n344\t  };\n345\t\n346\t  return {\n347\t    allow: filterBash(perms.allow),\n348\t    deny: filterBash(perms.deny),\n349\t    ask: filterBash(perms.ask),\n350\t  };\n351\t}\n352\t\n353\t/**\n354\t * Read Bash permission policies from up to 3 settings files.\n355\t *\n356\t * Returns policies in precedence order (most local first):\n357\t *   1. .claude/settings.local.json  (project-local)\n358\t *   2. .claude/settings.json        (project-shared)\n359\t *   3. ~/.claude/settings.json      (global)\n360\t *\n361\t * Missing or invalid files are silently skipped.\n362\t */\n363\texport function readBashPolicies(\n364\t  projectDir?: string,\n365\t  globalSettingsPath?: string,\n366\t): SecurityPolicy[] {\n367\t  const policies: SecurityPolicy[] = [];\n368\t\n369\t  if (projectDir) {\n370\t    const localPath = resolve(projectDir, \".claude\", \"settings.local.json\");\n371\t    const localPolicy = readSingleSettings(localPath);\n372\t    if (localPolicy) policies.push(localPolicy);\n373\t\n374\t    const sharedPath = resolve(projectDir, \".claude\", \"settings.json\");\n375\t    const sharedPolicy = readSingleSettings(sharedPath);\n376\t    if (sharedPolicy) policies.push(sharedPolicy);\n377\t  }\n378\t\n379\t  // Issue #451 round-3: read settings from EVERY adapter-specific global path\n380\t  // PLUS the claude global (defense in depth). When the caller passes an\n381\t  // explicit globalSettingsPath we honor it verbatim (back-compat with tests\n382\t  // and callers that already know which file to read).\n383\t  const globalPaths =\n384\t    globalSettingsPath !== undefined\n385\t      ? [globalSettingsPath]\n386\t      : resolveAdapterGlobalSettingsPaths();\n387\t\n388\t  for (const globalPath of globalPaths) {\n389\t    const globalPolicy = readSingleSettings(globalPath);\n390\t    if (globalPolicy) policies.push(globalPolicy);\n391\t  }\n392\t\n393\t  return policies;\n394\t}\n395\t\n396\t/**\n397\t * Read deny patterns for a specific tool from settings files.\n398\t *\n399\t * Reads the same 3-tier settings as `readBashPolicies`, but extracts\n400\t * only deny globs for the given tool. Used for Read and Grep enforcement\n401\t * — checks if file paths should be blocked by deny patterns.\n402\t *\n403\t * Returns an array of arrays (one per settings file, in precedence order).\n404\t * Each inner array contains the extracted glob strings.\n405\t */\n406\texport function readToolDenyPatterns(\n407\t  toolName: string,\n408\t  projectDir?: string,\n409\t  globalSettingsPath?: string,\n410\t): string[][] {\n411\t  return readToolPermissionPatterns(toolName, \"deny\", projectDir, globalSettingsPath);\n412\t}\n413\t\n414\t/**\n415\t * Read `permissions.{deny|allow}` globs for a tool from every settings file in\n416\t * precedence order (project local → project shared → adapter globals).\n417\t *\n418\t * Generalizes the original deny-only reader so the project-boundary guard\n419\t * (#852) can consult the SAME `permissions.allow` rules the user already\n420\t * maintains for the host's `Read` tool — instead of inventing a context-mode-\n421\t * specific opt-out env that would rot into dead code. A user who legitimately\n422\t * needs an out-of-project read expresses it once, in the host config, e.g.\n423\t * `\"permissions\": { \"allow\": [\"Read(/var/log/**)\"] }`, and both the host and\n424\t * context-mode honor it.\n425\t */\n426\texport function readToolPermissionPatterns(\n427\t  toolName: string,\n428\t  kind: \"deny\" | \"allow\",\n429\t  projectDir?: string,\n430\t  globalSettingsPath?: string,\n431\t): string[][] {\n432\t  const result: string[][] = [];\n433\t\n434\t  const extractGlobs = (path: string): string[] | null => {\n435\t    let raw: string;\n436\t    try {\n437\t      raw = readFileSync(path, \"utf-8\");\n438\t    } catch {\n439\t      return null;\n440\t    }\n441\t\n442\t    let parsed: any;\n443\t    try {\n444\t      parsed = JSON.parse(raw);\n445\t    } catch {\n446\t      return null;\n447\t    }\n448\t\n449\t    const entries = parsed?.permissions?.[kind];\n450\t    if (!Array.isArray(entries)) return [];\n451\t\n452\t    const globs: string[] = [];\n453\t    for (const entry of entries) {\n454\t      if (typeof entry !== \"string\") continue;\n455\t      const tp = parseToolPattern(entry);\n456\t      if (tp && tp.tool === toolName) {\n457\t        globs.push(tp.glob);\n458\t      }\n459\t    }\n460\t    return globs;\n461\t  };\n462\t\n463\t  if (projectDir) {\n464\t    const localGlobs = extractGlobs(\n465\t      resolve(projectDir, \".claude\", \"settings.local.json\"),\n466\t    );\n467\t    if (localGlobs !== null) result.push(localGlobs);\n468\t\n469\t    const sharedGlobs = extractGlobs(\n470\t      resolve(projectDir, \".claude\", \"settings.json\"),\n471\t    );\n472\t    if (sharedGlobs !== null) result.push(sharedGlobs);\n473\t  }\n474\t\n475\t  // Issue #451 round-3: union over every adapter-specific global path PLUS\n476\t  // claude global. Each settings file contributes its own globs array entry\n477\t  // so the precedence ordering downstream remains per-file rather than\n478\t  // collapsed.\n479\t  const globalPaths =\n480\t    globalSettingsPath !== undefined\n481\t      ? [globalSettingsPath]\n482\t      : resolveAdapterGlobalSettingsPaths();\n483\t\n484\t  for (const globalPath of globalPaths) {\n485\t    const globalGlobs = extractGlobs(globalPath);\n486\t    if (globalGlobs !== null) result.push(globalGlobs);\n487\t  }\n488\t\n489\t  return result;\n490\t}\n491\t\n492\t// ==============================================================================\n493\t// Decision Engine\n494\t// ==============================================================================\n495\t\n496\tinterface CommandDecision {\n497\t  decision: PermissionDecision;\n498\t  matchedPattern?: string;\n499\t}\n500\t\n501\t/**\n502\t * Evaluate a command against policies in precedence order.\n503\t *\n504\t * Splits chained commands (&&, ||, ;, |) and checks each segment\n505\t * against deny patterns — prevents bypassing deny by prepending\n506\t * innocent commands like \"echo ok && sudo rm -rf /\".\n507\t *\n508\t * Within each policy: deny > ask > allow (most restrictive wins).\n509\t * First definitive match across policies wins.\n510\t * Default (no match in any policy): \"ask\".\n511\t */\n512\texport function evaluateCommand(\n513\t  command: string,\n514\t  policies: SecurityPolicy[],\n515\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === \"win32\" || process.platform === \"darwin\",\n516\t): CommandDecision {\n517\t  // Extract all main segments and nested subshell commands\n518\t  const allCommands = collectCommandElements(command);\n519\t\n520\t  // 1. Deny check: If ANY segment or subshell command is denied, block the entire command\n521\t  for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {\n522\t    for (const policy of policies) {\n523\t      const denyMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.deny, caseInsensitive);\n524\t      if (denyMatch) return { decision: \"deny\", matchedPattern: denyMatch };\n525\t    }\n526\t  }\n527\t\n528\t  // 2. Allow/Ask check: Evaluate segment-by-segment in precedence order.\n529\t  // The command is allowed if and only if EVERY segment and subshell is explicitly allowed.\n530\t  // If any element matches an ask pattern or matches no allow pattern, it defaults to ask.\n531\t  for (const policy of policies) {\n532\t    let allAllowed = true;\n533\t    let anyAsk = false;\n534\t    let matchedAskPattern: string | undefined;\n535\t    let matchedAllowPattern: string | undefined;\n536\t\n537\t    for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {\n538\t      const askMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.ask, caseInsensitive);\n539\t      if (askMatch) {\n540\t        anyAsk = true;\n541\t        matchedAskPattern = askMatch;\n542\t        break; // Ask wins immediately within this policy\n543\t      }\n544\t\n545\t      const allowMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.allow, caseInsensitive);\n546\t      if (!allowMatch) {\n547\t        allAllowed = false;\n548\t      } else {\n549\t        matchedAllowPattern = allowMatch;\n550\t      }\n551\t    }\n552\t\n553\t    if (anyAsk) {\n554\t      return { decision: \"ask\", matchedPattern: matchedAskPattern };\n555\t    }\n556\t\n557\t    if (allAllowed && allCommands.length > 0) {\n558\t      return { decision: \"allow\", matchedPattern: matchedAllowPattern };\n559\t    }\n560\t  }\n561\t\n562\t  return { decision: \"ask\" };\n563\t}\n564\t\n565\t/**\n566\t * Server-side variant: only enforce deny patterns.\n567\t *\n568\t * The server has no UI for \"ask\" prompts, so allow/ask patterns are\n569\t * irrelevant. Returns \"deny\" if any deny pattern matches, otherwise \"allow\".\n570\t *\n571\t * Also splits chained commands and nested subshells to prevent bypass.\n572\t */\n573\texport function evaluateCommandDenyOnly(\n574\t  command: string,\n575\t  policies: SecurityPolicy[],\n576\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === \"win32\" || process.platform === \"darwin\",\n577\t): { decision: \"deny\" | \"allow\"; matchedPattern?: string } {\n578\t  const allCommands = collectCommandElements(command);\n579\t\n580\t  for (const cmdElement of allCommands) {\n581\t    for (const policy of policies) {\n582\t      const denyMatch = matchesAnyPattern(cmdElement, policy.deny, caseInsensitive);\n583\t      if (denyMatch) return { decision: \"deny\", matchedPattern: denyMatch };\n584\t    }\n585\t  }\n586\t\n587\t  return { decision: \"allow\" };\n588\t}\n589\t\n590\t// ==============================================================================\n591\t// File Path Evaluation\n592\t// ==============================================================================\n593\t\n594\t/**\n595\t * Check if a file path should be denied based on deny globs.\n596\t *\n597\t * Normalizes backslashes to forward slashes before matching so that\n598\t * Windows paths work with Unix-style glob patterns.\n599\t *\n600\t * When `projectRoot` is supplied, the path is also matched in its\n601\t * fully-resolved absolute form **and** — when the file exists — in\n602\t * its canonical form (`fs.realpathSync`). This prevents two classes\n603\t * of bypass:\n604\t *\n605\t *   1. `..` traversal: a relative path like `../../.ssh/id_rsa` no\n606\t *      longer evades absolute-path deny rules.\n607\t *   2. Symlink escape: a project-local path whose realpath points\n608\t *      outside the project (e.g. `safe.log -> ~/.ssh/id_rsa`) no\n609\t *      longer evades absolute-path deny rules.\n610\t *\n611\t * realpath is best-effort: if the file does not exist yet (ENOENT)\n612\t * or the syscall fails for any reason, the lexical resolved form is\n613\t * still checked. This keeps the function usable for paths that will\n614\t * be created during execution.\n615\t */\n616\texport function evaluateFilePath(\n617\t  filePath: string,\n618\t  denyGlobs: string[][],\n619\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === \"win32\" || process.platform === \"darwin\",\n620\t  projectRoot?: string,\n621\t): { denied: boolean; matchedPattern?: string } {\n622\t  const toForward = (path: string): string => path.replace(/\\\\/g, \"/\");\n623\t\n624\t  // Match against the raw input, the lexically-resolved absolute path,\n625\t  // and the canonical (symlink-resolved) path when the file exists.\n626\t  // Deduplicated so absolute inputs and paths that don't cross symlinks\n627\t  // don't pay the matching cost multiple times.\n628\t  const candidates = new Set<string>();\n629\t  candidates.add(toForward(filePath));\n630\t  if (projectRoot) {\n631\t    const lexical = resolve(projectRoot, filePath);\n632\t    candidates.add(toForward(lexical));\n633\t    try {\n634\t      candidates.add(toForward(realpathSync(lexical)));\n635\t    } catch {\n636\t      // File does not exist yet, or realpath failed — rely on lexical form.\n637\t    }\n638\t  }\n639\t\n640\t  for (const globs of denyGlobs) {\n641\t    for (const glob of globs) {\n642\t      // Normalize the glob's path separators the same way candidates were\n643\t      // normalized — otherwise a Windows absolute deny rule like\n644\t      // `Read(C:\\Users\\...\\secret.env)` parses with literal backslashes that\n645\t      // never match a forward-slash candidate.\n646\t      const regex = fileGlobToRegex(toForward(glob), caseInsensitive);\n647\t      for (const candidate of candidates) {\n648\t        if (regex.test(candidate)) {\n649\t          return { denied: true, matchedPattern: glob };\n650\t        }\n651\t      }\n652\t    }\n653\t  }\n654\t\n655\t  return { denied: false };\n656\t}\n657\t\n658\t// ==============================================================================\n659\t// Project-Boundary Containment (Issue #852)\n660\t// ==============================================================================\n661\t\n662\t/**\n663\t * Pure, algorithmic (no-regex) test: does `filePath` resolve to a location\n664\t * inside `projectRoot`?\n665\t *\n666\t * Issue #852 — `ctx_execute_file` previously fed its `path` argument straight\n667\t * into `resolve(projectRoot, path)`. Because `path.resolve` lets an *absolute*\n668\t * argument win outright, an agent could read any file on the host\n669\t * (`/home/user/secret`, `/etc/passwd`) regardless of the project root, and\n670\t * `../` traversal escaped just as easily. Claude Code's harness sandbox cannot\n671\t * inspect MCP input params, so the user approving the MCP call could not see\n672\t * that the path escaped the workspace. This guard re-anchors the path to the\n673\t * project boundary.\n674\t *\n675\t * Containment is decided on the *resolved* form. When the file (or its parent\n676\t * chain) exists, the symlink-canonical form is ALSO required to stay inside —\n677\t * this closes the symlink-escape class (a project-local `safe.log` whose\n678\t * realpath points at `~/.ssh/id_rsa`), mirroring `evaluateFilePath`.\n679\t *\n680\t * A path equal to the project root itself counts as inside. Comparison is\n681\t * case-insensitive on Windows/macOS to match those filesystems' semantics.\n682\t *\n683\t * Returns `true` when `projectRoot` is falsy (no boundary to enforce) so the\n684\t * caller's fail-open posture is preserved when the root cannot be resolved.\n685\t */\n686\texport function isPathInsideProject(\n687\t  filePath: string,\n688\t  projectRoot: string | undefined,\n689\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === \"win32\" || process.platform === \"darwin\",\n690\t): boolean {\n691\t  if (!projectRoot) return true;\n692\t\n693\t  const root = resolve(projectRoot);\n694\t  const lexical = resolve(projectRoot, filePath);\n695\t\n696\t  const within = (root: string, candidate: string): boolean => {\n697\t    let a = root;\n698\t    let b = candidate;\n699\t    if (caseInsensitive) {\n700\t      a = a.toLowerCase();\n701\t      b = b.toLowerCase();\n702\t    }\n703\t    if (a === b) return true;\n704\t    // `path.relative` is pure string arithmetic — no regex. A candidate inside\n705\t    // the root yields a relative path that neither starts with `..` (escapes\n706\t    // upward) nor is absolute (a different drive/root on Windows that cannot be\n707\t    // expressed relatively).\n708\t    const rel = relative(a, b);\n709\t    if (rel === \"\") return true;\n710\t    if (rel === \"..\" || rel.startsWith(\"..\" + sep)) return false;\n711\t    if (isAbsoluteRel(rel)) return false;\n712\t    return true;\n713\t  };\n714\t\n715\t  // Lexical containment is the primary gate.\n716\t  if (!within(root, lexical)) return false;\n717\t\n718\t  // Defense-in-depth: when the path (or a parent) is a symlink that points\n719\t  // outside the project, the canonical form must ALSO stay inside. Best-effort\n720\t  // — a not-yet-created file (ENOENT) falls back to the lexical decision above.\n721\t  try {\n722\t    const canonicalRoot = realpathSync(root);\n723\t    const canonical = realpathSync(lexical);\n724\t    if (!within(canonicalRoot, canonical)) return false;\n725\t  } catch {\n726\t    /* file does not exist yet / realpath failed — lexical decision stands */\n727\t  }\n728\t\n729\t  return true;\n730\t}\n731\t\n732\t/** Pure helper: is a `path.relative` result an absolute path? (no regex) */\n733\tfunction isAbsoluteRel(rel: string): boolean {\n734\t  if (rel.startsWith(\"/\")) return true; // POSIX absolute\n735\t  // Windows drive-absolute: \"C:\\...\" or \"C:/...\"\n736\t  if (rel.length >= 3 && rel[1] === \":\" && (rel[2] === \"\\\\\" || rel[2] === \"/\")) {\n737\t    const c = rel.charCodeAt(0);\n738\t    return (c >= 65 && c <= 90) || (c >= 97 && c <= 122);\n739\t  }\n740\t  return false;\n741\t}\n742\t\n743\t/**\n744\t * Decide whether `filePath` may be processed, given the project boundary AND\n745\t * the user's existing host `Read(...)` allow rules.\n746\t *\n747\t * Decision order:\n748\t *   1. Inside the project root  → allowed (the common case; no config needed).\n749\t *   2. Outside the project, but matching a `permissions.allow` `Read(...)` glob\n750\t *      the user already configured for the host → allowed. This is the\n751\t *      principled escape hatch: a deliberate out-of-project read is expressed\n752\t *      ONCE in the host config the user already maintains, reusing the same\n753\t *      mechanism Claude Code itself uses to whitelist a path outside the\n754\t *      sandbox — no context-mode-specific opt-out env that would rot into\n755\t *      dead code.\n756\t *   3. Outside the project, no allow match → denied (closes the #852 escape).\n757\t *\n758\t * `allowGlobs` has the same per-settings-file shape as the deny globs returned\n759\t * by `readToolPermissionPatterns(toolName, \"allow\", …)`. Allow-matching reuses\n760\t * `evaluateFilePath` so absolute/`..`/symlink-canonical candidate resolution is\n761\t * identical to the deny path — one matcher, no divergence.\n762\t *\n763\t * Fail-open on an unknown project root (boundary cannot be computed) so the\n764\t * guard never blocks legitimate in-project work when resolution fails.\n765\t */\n766\texport function evaluateProjectContainment(\n767\t  filePath: string,\n768\t  projectRoot: string | undefined,\n769\t  allowGlobs: string[][] = [],\n770\t  caseInsensitive: boolean = process.platform === \"win32\" || process.platform === \"darwin\",\n771\t): { allowed: boolean; reason: \"inside\" | \"allow-rule\" | \"outside\" } {\n772\t  if (isPathInsideProject(filePath, projectRoot, caseInsensitive)) {\n773\t    return { allowed: true, reason: \"inside\" };\n774\t  }\n775\t  // Outside the project — permit only if the user explicitly allowed this path\n776\t  // for the host Read tool. `evaluateFilePath` returns `denied:true` when a glob\n777\t  // MATCHES, so a match here means \"explicitly allowed\".\n778\t  if (allowGlobs.some((g) => g.length > 0)) {\n779\t    const matched = evaluateFilePath(filePath, allowGlobs, caseInsensitive, projectRoot);\n780\t    if (matched.denied) return { allowed: true, reason: \"allow-rule\" };\n781\t  }\n782\t  return { allowed: false, reason: \"outside\" };\n783\t}\n784\t\n785\t// ==============================================================================\n786\t// Shell-Escape Scanner\n787\t// ==============================================================================\n788\t\n789\t// Regex patterns that detect shell-escape calls in non-shell languages.\n790\t// Each pattern uses capture groups so that the embedded command string\n791\t// can be extracted from the last non-quote group.\n792\t//\n793\t// NOTE: These regexes contain literal strings like \"execSync\" — they are\n794\t// patterns for *detecting* shell escapes in user code, not actual usage.\n795\tconst SHELL_ESCAPE_PATTERNS: Record<string, RegExp[]> = {\n796\t  python: [\n797\t    /os\\.system\\(\\s*(['\"])(.*?)\\1\\s*\\)/g,\n798\t    /subprocess\\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\\(\\s*(['\"])(.*?)\\1/g,\n799\t  ],\n800\t  javascript: [\n801\t    /exec(?:Sync|File|FileSync)?\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n802\t    /spawn(?:Sync)?\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n803\t  ],\n804\t  typescript: [\n805\t    /exec(?:Sync|File|FileSync)?\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n806\t    /spawn(?:Sync)?\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n807\t  ],\n808\t  ruby: [\n809\t    /system\\(\\s*(['\"])(.*?)\\1/g,\n810\t    /`(.*?)`/g,\n811\t  ],\n812\t  go: [\n813\t    /exec\\.Command\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n814\t  ],\n815\t  php: [\n816\t    /shell_exec\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n817\t    /(?:^|[^.])exec\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n818\t    /(?:^|[^.])system\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n819\t    /passthru\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n820\t    /proc_open\\(\\s*(['\"`])(.*?)\\1/g,\n821\t subprocess.run([\"rm\", \"-rf\", \"/\"]) → \"rm -rf /\"\n831\t *\n832\t * This catches the list-of-strings form that the single-string regex misses.\n833\t */\n834\tfunction extractPythonSubprocessListArgs(code: string): string[] {\n835\t  const commands: string[] = [];\n836\t  const pattern =\n837\t    /subprocess\\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\\(\\s*\\[([^\\]]+)\\]/g;\n838\t\n839\t  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;\n840\t  while ((match = pattern.exec(code)) !== null) {\n841\t    const listContent = match[1];\n842\t    const args = [...listContent.matchAll(/(['\"])(.*?)\\1/g)].map((m) => m[2]);\n843\t    if (args.length > 0) {\n844\t      commands.push(args.join(\" *\n855\t * Returns an array of command strings found in the code. For unknown\n856\t * languages or code without shell-escape calls, returns an empty array.\n857\t */\n858\texport function extractShellCommands(\n859\t  code: string,\n860\t  language: string,\n861\t): string[] {\n862\t  const patterns = SHELL_ESCAPE_PATTERNS[language];\n863\t  if (!patterns && language !== \"python\") return [];\n864\t\n865\t  const commands: string[] = [];\n866\t\n867\t  if (patterns) {\n868\t    for (const pattern of patterns) {\n869\t      // Reset lastIndex since we reuse the global regex\n870\t      pattern.lastIndex = 0;\n871\t\n872\t      let match: RegExpExecArray | null;\n873\t      while ((match = pattern.exec(code)) !== null) {\n874\t        // The command string is in the last capture group that isn't the\n875\t        // quote delimiter. For patterns with 2 groups (quote + content),\n876\t        // it's group 2. When the user's request is approximate, pick a minute that is NOT 0 or 30:\n  \"every morning around 9\" → \"57 8 * * *\" or \"3 9 * * *\" (not \"0 9 * * *\")\n  \"hourly\" → \"7 * * * *\" (not \"0 * * * *\")\n  \"in an hour or so, remind me to...\" → pick whatever minute you land on, don't round\n\nOnly use minute 0 or 30 when the user names that exact time and clearly means it (\"at 9:00 sharp\", \"at half past\", coordinating with a meeting). On session exit, if still in the worktree, the user will be prompted to keep or remove it\n\n## Entering an existing worktree\n\nPass `path` instead of `name` to switch the session into a worktree that already exists (e.g., one you just created with `git worktree add`). ExitWorktree will not remove a worktree entered this way; use `action: \"keep\"` to return to the original directory.\n\nSwitching with `path` also works when the session is already in a worktree (the previous worktree is left on disk, untouched, and only the new one is tracked for exit-time cleanup), and from agents whose working directory was pinned at launch (subagent isolation or explicit cwd). In both cases the target must be a worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` of the same repository, and from a pinned agent the switch only affects this agent, not the parent session. After a further switch, previously-visited worktrees are no longer writable — re-issue EnterWorktree with `path` to return to one.\n\n## Parameters\n\n- `name` (optional): A name for a new worktree. It will NOT touch:\n- Worktrees you created manually with `git worktree add`\n- Worktrees from a previous session (even if created by EnterWorktree then)\n- The directory you're in if EnterWorktree was never called\n\nIf called outside an EnterWorktree session, the tool is a **no-op**: it reports that no worktree session is active and takes no action. Use this for a clean exit when the work is done or abandoned.\n- `discard_changes` (optional, default false): only meaningful with `action: \"remove\"`. If the worktree has uncommitted files or commits not on the original branch, the tool will REFUSE to remove it unless this is set to `true`.           "name": "ScheduleWakeup",
          "description": "Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use the default 5-minute Anthropic prompt-cache TTL. Sleeping past 300 seconds means the next wake-up reads your full conversation context uncached — slower and more expensive. So the natural breakpoints:\n\n- **Under 5 minutes (60s–270s)**: cache stays warm. Right for actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about — a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue.\n- **5 minutes to 1 hour (300s–3600s)**: pay the cache miss. Right when there's no point checking sooner — waiting on something that takes minutes to change, genuinely idle, or as the long fallback heartbeat when something else is the primary wake signal.\n\n**Don't pick 300s.** It's the worst-of-both: you pay the cache miss without amortizing it. If you're tempted to \"wait 5 minutes,\" either drop to 270s (stay in cache) or commit to 1200s+ (one cache miss buys a much longer wait). Don't think in round-number minutes — think in cache windows.\n\nFor idle ticks with no specific signal to watch, default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop checks back, you don't burn cache 12× per hour for nothing, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nThink about what you're actually waiting for, not just \"how long should I sleep.\" If you're polling a CI run that takes ~8 minutes, sleeping 60s burns the cache 8 times before it finishes — sleep ~270s twice instead.\n\nThe runtime clamps to [60, 3600], so you don't need to clamp yourself.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n",

        {
          "name": "Workflow",
          "description": "Execute a workflow script that orchestrates multiple subagents deterministically. Workflows run in the background — this tool returns immediately with a task ID, and a <task-notification> arrives when the workflow completes. Use /workflows to watch live progress.\n\nA workflow structures work across many agents — to be comprehensive (decompose and cover in parallel), to be confident (independent perspectives and adversarial checks before committing), or to take on scale one context can't hold (migrations, audits, broad sweeps). The script is where you encode that structure: what fans out, what verifies, what synthesizes.\n\nONLY call this tool when the user has explicitly opted into multi-agent orchestration. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** below.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nWhen you do call it, the right move is often **hybrid**: scout inline first (list the files, find the channels, scope the diff) to discover the work-list, then call Workflow to pipeline over it. You don't need to know the shape before the *task* — only before the *orchestration step*.\n\nCommon single-phase workflows you can chain across turns:\n- **Understand** — parallel readers over relevant subsystems → structured map\n- **Design** — judge panel of N independent approaches → scored synthesis\n- **Review** — dimensions → find → adversarially verify (example below)\n- **Research** — multi-modal sweep → deep-read → synthesize\n- **Migrate** — discover sites → transform each (worktree isolation) → verify\n\nFor larger work, run several in sequence — read each result before deciding the next phase. You stay in the loop; each workflow is one well-scoped fan-out.\n\n**Ultracode.** When a system-reminder confirms ultracode is on, that opt-in is standing: author and run a workflow for every substantive task by default. The goal is the most exhaustive, correct answer you can produce — token cost is not a constraint. For multi-phase work (understand → design → implement → review), that often means several workflows in sequence — one per phase — so you stay in the loop between them. The quality patterns below (adversarial verify, multi-modal sweep, completeness critic, loop-until-dry) are the tools; pick what fits the task. Lean toward orchestrating with workflows and adversarially verifying your findings — unless the work is trivial or already verified. Solo only on conversational turns or trivial mechanical edits. When a reminder says ultracode is off, revert to the opt-in rule above.\n\nPass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first. Every invocation automatically persists its script to a file under the session directory and returns the path in the tool result. To iterate on a workflow, edit that file with Write/Edit and re-invoke Workflow with `{scriptPath: \"<path>\"}` instead of resending the full script.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'find-flaky-tests',\n    description: 'Find flaky tests and propose fixes',   // one-line, shown in permission dialog\n    phases: [                                            // one entry per phase() call\n      { title: 'Scan', detail: 'grep test logs for retries' },\n      { title: 'Fix', detail: 'one agent per flaky test' },\n    ],\n  }\n  // script body starts here — use agent()/parallel()/pipeline()/phase()/log()\n  phase('Scan')\n  const flaky = await agent('grep CI logs for retry markers', {schema: FLAKY_SCHEMA})\n  ...\n\nThe `meta` object must be a PURE LITERAL — no variables, function calls, spreads, or template interpolation. Required fields: `name`, `description`. Optional: `whenToUse` (shown in the workflow list), `phases`. Use the SAME phase titles in meta.phases as in phase() calls — titles are matched exactly; a phase() call with no matching meta entry just gets its own progress group. Add `model` to a phase entry when that phase uses a specific model override.\n\nScript body hooks:\n- agent(prompt: string, opts?: {label?: string, phase?: string, schema?: object, model?: string, effort?: string, isolation?: 'worktree', agentType?: string}): Promise<any> — spawn a subagent. Without schema, returns its final text as a string. With schema (a JSON Schema), the subagent is forced to call a StructuredOutput tool and agent() returns the validated object — no parsing needed. Returns null if the user skips the agent mid-run or the subagent dies on a terminal API error after retries (filter with .filter(Boolean)). opts.label overrides the display label. opts.phase explicitly assigns this agent to a progress group (use this inside pipeline()/parallel() stages to avoid races on the global phase() state — same phase string → same group box). opts.model overrides the model for this agent call. Default to omitting it — the agent inherits the main-loop model (the resolved session model), which is almost always correct. Only set it when you're highly confident a different tier fits the task; when unsure, omit. opts.effort overrides the reasoning effort for this agent call ('low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max') — omit to inherit the session effort; use 'low' for cheap mechanical stages and higher tiers only for the hardest verify/judge stages. opts.isolation: 'worktree' runs the agent in a fresh git worktree — EXPENSIVE (~200-500ms setup + disk per agent), use ONLY when agents mutate files in parallel and would otherwise conflict; the worktree is auto-removed if unchanged. opts.agentType uses a custom subagent type (e.g. 'general-purpose', 'code-reviewer') instead of the default workflow subagent — resolved from the same registry as the Agent tool; composes with schema (the custom agent's system prompt gets a StructuredOutput instruction appended).\n- pipeline(items, stage1, stage2, ...): Promise<any[]> — run each item through all stages independently, NO barrier between stages. Item A can be in stage 3 while item B is still in stage 1. This is the DEFAULT for multi-stage work. Wall-clock = slowest single-item chain, not sum-of-slowest-per-stage. Every stage callback receives (prevResult, originalItem, index) — use originalItem/index in later stages to label work without threading context through stage 1's return value. A stage that throws drops that item to `null` and skips its remaining stages.\n- parallel(thunks: Array<() => Promise<any>>): Promise<any[]> — run tasks concurrently. This is a BARRIER: awaits all thunks before returning. A thunk that throws (or whose agent errors) resolves to `null` in the result array — the call itself never rejects, so `.filter(Boolean)` before using the results. Use ONLY when you genuinely need all results together.\n- log(message: string): void — emit a progress message to the user (shown as a narrator line above the progress tree)\n- phase(title: string): void — start a new phase; subsequent agent() calls are grouped under this title in the progress display\n- args: any — the value passed as Workflow's `args` input, verbatim (undefined if not provided). Pass arrays/objects as actual JSON values in the tool call, NOT as a JSON-encoded string — `args: [\"a.ts\", \"b.ts\"]`, not `args: \"[\\\"a.ts\\\", ...]\"` (a stringified list reaches the script as one string, so `args.filter`/`args.map` throw). Use this to parameterize named workflows — e.g. pass a research question, target path, or config object directly instead of via a side-channel file.\n- budget: {total: number|null, spent(): number, remaining(): number} — the turn's token target from the user's \"+500k\"-style directive. `budget.total` is null if no target was set. `budget.spent()` returns output tokens spent this turn across the main loop and all workflows — the pool is shared, not per-workflow. `budget.remaining()` returns `max(0, total - spent())`, or `Infinity` if no target. The target is a HARD ceiling, not advisory: once `spent()` reaches `total`, further `agent()` calls throw. Use for dynamic loops: `while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) { ... }`, or static scaling: `const FLEET = budget.total ? Math.floor(budget.total / 100_000) : 5`.\n- workflow(nameOrRef: string | {scriptPath: string}, args?: any): Promise<any> — run another workflow inline as a sub-step and return whatever it returns. Pass a name to invoke a saved workflow (same registry as {name: \"...\"}), or {scriptPath} to run a script file you Wrote earlier. The child shares this run's concurrency cap, agent counter, abort signal, and token budget — its agents appear under a \"▸ name\" group in /workflows and its tokens count toward budget.spent(). The args param becomes the child's `args` global. Nesting is one level only: workflow() inside a child throws. Throws on unknown name / unreadable scriptPath / child syntax error; catch to handle gracefully.\n\nSubagents are told their final text IS the return value (not a human-facing message), so they return raw data. For structured output, use the schema option — validation happens at the tool-call layer so the model retries on mismatch.\n\nWorkflow agents can reach all session-connected MCP tools via ToolSearch — schemas load on demand per agent. Caveat: interactively-authenticated MCP servers (e.g. claude.ai) may be absent in headless/cron runs.\n\nScripts are plain JavaScript, NOT TypeScript — type annotations (`: string[]`), interfaces, and generics fail to parse. The script body runs in an async context — use await directly. Standard JS built-ins (JSON, Math, Array, etc.) are available — EXCEPT `Date.now()`/`Math.random()`/argless `new Date()`, which throw (they would break resume); pass timestamps in via `args`, stamp results after the workflow returns, and for randomness vary the agent prompt/label by index. No filesystem or Node.js API access.\n\nDEFAULT TO pipeline(). Only reach for a barrier (parallel between stages) when you genuinely need ALL prior-stage results together.\n\nA barrier is correct ONLY when stage N needs cross-item context from all of stage N-1:\n- Dedup/merge across the full result set before expensive downstream work\n- Early-exit if the total count is zero (\"0 bugs found → skip verification entirely\")\n- Stage N's prompt references \"the other findings\" for comparison\n\nA barrier is NOT justified by:\n- \"I need to flatten/map/filter first\" — do it inside a pipeline stage: pipeline(items, stageA, r => transform([r]).flat(), stageB)\n- \"The stages are conceptually separate\" — that's what pipeline() models. Separate stages ≠ synchronized stages.\n- \"It's cleaner code\" — barrier latency is real. If 5 finders run and the slowest takes 3× the fastest, a barrier wastes 2/3 of the fast finders' idle time.\n\nSmell test: if you wrote\n  const a = await parallel(...)\n  const b = transform(a)        // flatten, map, filter — no cross-item dependency\n  const c = await parallel(b.map(...))\nthat middle transform doesn't need the barrier. Rewrite as a pipeline with the transform inside a stage. When in doubt: pipeline.\n\nConcurrent agent() calls are capped at min(16, cpu cores - 2) per workflow — excess calls queue and run as slots free up. You can still pass 100 items to parallel()/pipeline() and they all complete; only ~10 run at any moment. Total agent count across a workflow's lifetime is capped at 1000 — a runaway-loop backstop set far above any real workflow. A single parallel()/pipeline() call accepts at most 4096 items; passing more is an explicit error, not a silent truncation.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nWhen a barrier IS correct — dedup across all findings before expensive verification:\n  const all = await parallel(DIMENSIONS.map(d => () => agent(d.prompt, {schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA})))\n  const deduped = dedupeByFileAndLine(all.filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.findings))  // <-- genuinely needs ALL at once\n  const verified = await parallel(deduped.map(f => () => agent(verifyPrompt(f), {schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})))\n\nLoop-until-count pattern — accumulate to a target:\n  const bugs = []\n  while (bugs.length < 10) {\n    const result = await agent(\"Find bugs in this codebase.\", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})\n    bugs.push(...result.bugs)\n    log(`${bugs.length}/10 found`)\n  }\n\nLoop-until-budget pattern — scale depth to the user's \"+500k\" directive. Guard on budget.total: with no target set, remaining() is Infinity and the loop would run straight to the 1000-agent cap.\n  const bugs = []\n  while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) {\n    const result = await agent(\"Find bugs in this codebase.\", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})\n    bugs.push(...result.bugs)\n    log(`${bugs.length} found, ${Math.round(budget.remaining()/1000)}k remaining`)\n  }\n\nComposing patterns — exhaustive review (find → dedup vs seen → diverse-lens panel → loop-until-dry):\n  const seen = new Set(), confirmed = []\n  let dry = 0\n  while (dry < 2) {                                              // loop-until-dry\n    const found = (await parallel(FINDERS.map(f => () =>          // barrier: collect all finders this round\n      agent(f.prompt, {phase: 'Find', schema: BUGS})))).filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.bugs)\n    const fresh = found.filter(b => !seen.has(key(b)))           // dedup vs ALL seen — plain code, not an agent\n    if (!fresh.length) { dry++; continue }\n    dry = 0; fresh.forEach(b => seen.add(key(b)))\n    const judged = await parallel(fresh.map(b => () =>           // every fresh bug judged concurrently...\n      parallel(['correctness','security','repro'].map(lens => () =>   // ...each by 3 distinct lenses\n        agent(`Judge \"${b.desc}\" via the ${lens} lens — real?`, {phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT})))\n        .then(vs => ({ b, real: vs.filter(Boolean).filter(v => v.real).length >= 2 }))))\n    confirmed.push(...judged.filter(v => v.real).map(v => v.b))\n  }\n  return confirmed\n  // dedup vs `seen`, NOT `confirmed` — else judge-rejected findings reappear every round and it never converges.\n\nQuality patterns — common shapes; pick by task and compose freely:\n- Adversarial verify: spawn N independent skeptics per finding, each prompted to REFUTE. Kill if ≥majority refute. Prevents plausible-but-wrong findings from surviving.\n    const votes = await parallel(Array.from({length: 3}, () => () =>\n      agent(`Try to refute: ${claim}. Default to refuted=true if uncertain.`, {schema: VERDICT})))\n    const survives = votes.filter(Boolean).filter(v => !v.refuted).length >= 2\n- Perspective-diverse verify: when a finding can fail in more than one way, give each verifier a distinct lens (correctness, security, perf, does-it-reproduce) instead of N identical refuters — diversity catches failure modes redundancy can't.\n- Judge panel: generate N independent attempts from different angles (e.g. MVP-first, risk-first, user-first), score with parallel judges, synthesize from the winner while grafting the best ideas from runners-up. Beats one-attempt-iterated when the solution space is wide.\n- Loop-until-dry: for unknown-size discovery (bugs, issues, edge cases), keep spawning finders until K consecutive rounds return nothing new. Simple counters (while count < N) miss the tail.\n- Multi-modal sweep: parallel agents each searching a different way (by-container, by-content, by-entity, by-time). Each is blind to what the others surface; useful when one search angle won't find everything.\n- Completeness critic: a final agent that asks \"what's missing — modality not run, claim unverified, source unread?\" What it finds becomes the next round of work.\n- No silent caps: if a workflow bounds coverage (top-N, no-retry, sampling), `log()` what was dropped — silent truncation reads as \"covered everything\" when it didn't.\n\nScale to what the user asked for. \"find any bugs\" → a few finders, single-vote verify. \"thoroughly audit this\" or \"be comprehensive\" → larger finder pool, 3–5 vote adversarial pass, synthesis stage. When unsure, lean toward thoroughness for research/review/audit requests and toward brevity for quick checks.\n\nThese patterns aren't exhaustive — compose novel harnesses when the task calls for it (tournament brackets, self-repair loops, staged escalation, whatever fits).\n\nUse this tool for multi-step orchestration where control flow should be deterministic (loops, conditionals, fan-out) rather than model-driven.\n\n## Resume\n\nThe tool result includes a runId. To resume after a pause, kill, or script edit, relaunch with Workflow({scriptPath, resumeFromRunId}) — the longest unchanged prefix of agent() calls returns cached results instantly; the first edited/new call and everything after it runs live. Same script + same args → 100% cache hit. Before diagnosing why a completed workflow returned an empty or unexpected result, Read <transcriptDir>/journal.jsonl — it records each agent's actual return value; do not assume cached results are non-empty. Date.now()/Math.random()/new Date() are unavailable in scripts (they would break this) — stamp results after the workflow returns, or pass timestamps via args. Fallback when no journal is available: Read agent-<id>.jsonl files in the transcript directory and hand-author a continuation script.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config.",
